at a point
p
. It can be characterized using Corollary
3.6.20
. This definition a priori does not need the property that
V(\overline{x}_{1})
contains the equimultiple locus and thus can be used in a nonzero characteristic. (See for instance Example
4.5.4
)

The definition is straightforward when no divisors are present. In such cases, ”maximal contact” refers to a maximal partial set of coordinates contained in
T^{1/a_{1}}({\mathcal{R}})
. However, in general, maximal contact at a point
p
is a partial set of coordinates that is compatible with an SNC divisor, consisting of free coordinates in
T^{1/a_{1}}({\mathcal{R}})
along with the divisorial coordinates contained in
T^{1/a_{1}}({\mathcal{R}})+m_{p}^{2}
, thus tangential to
T^{1/a_{1}}({\mathcal{R}})
.

The concept is specifically designed to enhance the efficiency of computations by eliminating unnecessary steps. Unlike in the standard approach, the notion incorporates multiple maximal contact coordinates simultaneously, which simplifies the representation of the main invariant and significantly aids in its computation. The idea of utilizing multiple maximal contacts is well-known among experts and is commonly employed in practical implementations for its effectiveness.

Definition 3.6.11
.

Given a Rees algebra
R
on
X
and a rational number
a_{1}>0
. By a
partial maximal contact of
(R,a_{1})
on an open affine subset
U
we mean a partial system of coordinates on
U
compatible with
E
:

\overline{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},x_{s+1},\ldots,x_{r}),

such that

(1)

If
i\leq s
then
x_{i}\in T^{1/a_{1}}(R)(U)
and
x_{i}
is free on
U
. In particular
\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}(T^{1/a_{1}}R)={\mathcal{O}}_{U}
.

(2)

If
s+1\leq i\leq r
, then
V(x_{i})\in E
, and
\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}(T^{1/a_{1}}R)={\mathcal{O}}_{U}
,

If
p\in V(\overline{x})
, and
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R)=a_{1}
, and the conditions (1) and (2) are satisfied in a neighborhood of a point
p
then
\overline{x}
is a
partial maximal contact
of
R
at
p
.

If additionally

\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}(T^{1/a_{1}}R)\neq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
at
p
for
j>r
, where
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
is a local system of parameters extending
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})
then we say that
\overline{x}
is a
maximal contact
of
R
at
p
.

We shall associate partial maximal contact with respect to
(R,a)
gradation
t^{1/a_{1}}
and write it in the graded form
\overline{x}t^{1/a_{1}}
.

Lemma 3.6.12
.

Let
\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}
be a maximal contact for
R
at
p
, with
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R)=a_{1}
. Then it is a partial maximal contact for
(R,a_{1})
in a certain neighborhood
U
of
p
. ∎

Proof.

Let us consider an open neighborhood where the conditions (1) and (2) of Definition
3.6.11
are met.
∎

Remark 3.6.13
.

Note that a partial maximal contact on
U
exist if
a_{1}
is an integer such that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R)\leq a_{1}
for all
p\in U
. More specifically we have:

Lemma 3.6.14
.

If
\overline{x}
is a partial maximal contact of
(R,a_{1})
on
U
, then
{\operatorname{ord}}_{q}(R)\leq a_{1}
for any
q\in U
.

Proof.

It follows from the definition of the partial maximal contact that
{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(T^{1/a_{1}}R)={\mathcal{O}}_{U}
.
As a consequence, for every point
q
in
U
, there exists
{\mathcal{D}}_{\overline{x}^{\alpha}}(f_{j})
that is invertible at
q
, where
f_{j}t^{b_{j}}
is a generator of
R
and
|\alpha|=b_{j}a_{1}
.

∎

Example 3.6.15
.

Let

R={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(x^{3}+y^{5}+z^{7})t,(v^{4}w^{2})t^{2}]

where
a_{1}=3={\operatorname{ord}}_{0}(R)
, as in Example
3.6.6
.

Then
T^{1/3}(R)t^{1/3}=(x,y^{3},z^{4},v,w)t^{1/3},
and the maximal contact at
0
is given by
(x,v,w)t^{1/3}
in gradation
t^{1/3}
. It is a partial maximal contact for
(R,3)
on
X
.

Lemma 3.6.16
.

(1)

A maximal contact of
R
at
p\in X
exists.

(2)

Any partial maximal contact of
R
at
p
can be extended to a maximal contact
of
R
at
p
.

(3)

The image of a maximal contact
\overline{x}
at
p
in
m_{p}/m_{p}^{2}
is determined uniquely. It is the smallest subspace compatible with
E_{p}
containing the image of
T^{1/a_{1}}_{p}(R)
.

(4)

The divisorial coordinates in the maximal contact
\overline{x}
at
p
are uniquely determined and do not depend upon the maximal contact.

Proof.

According to the definition, for a given
a
, we have
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R_{a})=aa_{1}
. Consequently,
D^{aa_{1}-1}(R_{a})\subset T^{1/a_{1}}R
, and
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(D^{aa_{1}-1}(R_{a}))={\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(T^{1/a_{1}}R)=1
.

Let
{\mathcal{I}}_{E,p}
denote the ideal generated by the divisorial coordinates in
E_{p}
(i.e., in
E
passing through
p
).
Consider the image

\overline{T}^{a_{1}}R:=(T^{1/a_{1}}R+{\mathcal{I}}_{E,p}+m_{p}^{2})/({\mathcal{I}}_{E,p}+m_{p}^{2})

of
T^{1/a_{1}}R
in
m_{p}/({\mathcal{I}}_{E,p}+m_{p}^{2})
.
Its basis is determined by a partial system of local free parameters
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in T^{1/a_{1}}(R)
. Then we can extend it to a partial coordinate system

\overline{x}:=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},x_{s+1},\ldots,x_{r}),

where
x_{i}
, for
i\geq s+1
are divisorial at
p
with the smallest image in
m_{p}/m_{p}^{2}
containing the image of
T^{1/a_{1}}(R)
.
Consequently, for any divisorial
x_{j}
, there exists an element
v=\sum c_{i}x_{i}\in T^{1/a_{1}}+m_{p}^{2}
with
c_{j}\neq 0
at
p
, and all
x_{i}
are divisorial.

Furthermore,
\overline{x}
precisely represents a maximal contact of
R
at
p
. Conversely, any maximal contact of
R
at
p
can be constructed in this manner.

If
\overline{x}
is any partial maximal contact, its image in
\overline{T}^{1/a_{1}}R
can be extended to form a basis for
\overline{T}^{1/a_{1}}R
. This allows us to extend the free part of
\overline{x}
to a maximal contact
\overline{x^{\prime}}
. We need to show that
\overline{x^{\prime}}
contains the entirety of
\overline{x}
.

If
x_{j}\in\overline{x}
is a divisorial coordinate, according to the previous discussion,
D_{x_{j}}(v)\neq 0
, where
v\in T^{1/a_{1}}(R)
and
v=\sum a_{i}x_{i},({\rm mod}\,\,m_{p}^{2})
, with
x_{i}
all being divisorial coordinates that include
x_{j}
. As the image of the maximal contact
\overline{x^{\prime}}
in
m_{p}/m_{p}^{2}
necessarily includes the image of
v
, it also contains the images of all
x_{i}
with
a_{i}\neq 0
, including the coordinate
x_{j}
. Hence,
\overline{x}\subseteq\overline{x^{\prime}}
.

In conclusion, the divisorial coordinates are precisely those that appear in the presentation of some
v\in T^{1/a_{1}}(R)
, where
v=\sum a_{i}x_{i},({\rm mod}\,m_{p}^{2})
, with all
x_{i}
being divisorial. Therefore, the divisorial coordinates are uniquely determined.

∎

Lemma 3.6.17
.

Let
R\subset{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{Int}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{\operatorname{Int}}
at a point
p\in X
such that

a_{1}={\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R)
. Then upon the change of the coordinate representation of
{\mathcal{A}}
,
\overline{x}_{1}
contains a maximal contact
\overline{x}
of
R
at
p
.

Proof.

According to Lemma
3.4.3
, the operators
t^{-|\alpha|/a_{1}}{\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\alpha}}
in the definition of
T^{1/a_{1}}(R)
preserve
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{Int}}
. Therefore, we have the following inclusion:

R\subset{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{Int}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{\operatorname{Int}}

implies that

T^{1/a_{1}}(R)t^{1/a_{1}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{Int}}
.
Consider the free part
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})
of the maximal contact
\overline{x}
in
T^{1/a_{1}}(R)
. According to Lemma
3.1.29
, we can assume that

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})t^{1/a_{1}}\subseteq\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}\subseteq{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{1/a_{1}},

after a coordinate change. Since
a_{i}>a_{1}
for
i\geq 2
,the image of
\overline{x}_{1}
in
m_{p}/m_{p}^{2}
is the same as the image of
{\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{Int}}}_{1/a_{1}}
in
m_{p}/m_{p}^{2}
and includes the image of
T^{1/a_{1}}(R)
. By Lemma
3.6.16
, it also includes the image of the maximal contact
\overline{x}
of
R
. Moreover,
\overline{x}_{1}
is compatible with the divisorial structure, indicating that it must also contain the divisorial part of the maximal contact itself. Therefore,
\overline{x}_{1}\supseteq\overline{x}
.

∎

Definition 3.6.18
.

To a partial maximal contact
\overline{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})
of
R
on
U
, we can associate the invariant

{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(\overline{x})=(1,\ldots,1,1_{+},\ldots 1_{+}),

where the
1
’s correspond to the free maximal coordinates in
\overline{x}
, and the
1_{+}
’s correspond to the coordinates in
\overline{x}
that define the divisors on
U
.

Lemma 3.6.19
.

Given a Rees algebra
R
of order
a_{1}
at
p\in X

with a maximal contact

\overline{x}
of
R
at
p
.
Then

(1)

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}^{1}(R)=a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x}).

(2)

R\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}t^{1/a_{1}},\overline{y}t^{1/a_{2}}]^{\operatorname{Int}},
for some
a_{2}>a_{1}
, and a coordinate system
(\overline{x},\overline{y})
at
p
compatible with
E
.

(3)

If
\overline{x}
is a partial maximal contact for
(R,a_{1})
on
U
then
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}^{1}(R)\leq a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x})
.

Proof.

(1) and (2). If
R\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}])
at
p
, where
a_{1}={\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R)
, then according to Lemma
3.6.17
,
\overline{x}_{1}
contains a maximal contact of
R
after a coordinate change. Therefore, based on Definition
3.6.18
, we have
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}(R)\leq a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x})
.

Moreover, note that since
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R)\geq a_{1}
, we have
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(C_{\overline{x}t^{1/a_{1}}}R)\geq a_{1}
and
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(C_{\overline{x}t^{1/a_{1}}}R)_{|H}\geq a_{1}
. Suppose the latter order is equal to
a_{1}
. Then, since
C_{\overline{x}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R)_{|H}
is generated by the coefficients as shown in Lemma
3.5.5
, there exists a generator

ft^{a}=\sum c_{\alpha}\overline{x}^{\alpha}t^{a}\in R_{a}t^{a}

of
R
, with
c_{\alpha}=c_{\alpha}(\overline{y}),
for the corresponding system of local parameters
(\overline{x},\overline{y})
,
such that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(c_{\alpha})=a_{1}a-|\alpha|
for some
\alpha
.
Moreover, for some
\beta
with
|\beta|={\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(c_{\alpha})=a_{1}a-|\alpha|
, the derivative
D_{\overline{y}^{\beta}}(c_{\alpha})=D_{y_{1}^{b_{1}},\ldots y_{k}^{b_{k}}}(c_{\alpha})
is invertible. Consequently,
D_{\overline{y}^{\beta}}D_{x^{\alpha}}(f)
is invertible, and we have
D_{y_{1}^{b_{1}-1},\ldots y_{k}^{b_{k}}}D_{x^{\alpha}}(f)\in T^{1/a_{1}}(R)
. Thus,
D_{y_{1}}(T^{1/a_{1}}(R))
is invertible, contradicting the condition of maximal contact at a point as defined in Definition
3.6.11
.

This implies that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(C_{\overline{x}t^{1/a_{1}}}R){|H}=a_{2}>a_{1}
. By Lemma
3.1.24
, this further implies that
C_{\overline{x}t^{1/a_{1}}|H}R\in{\mathcal{O}}_{H}[\overline{y}t^{1/a_{2}}]^{\operatorname{Int}}
. Hence, based on Lemma
3.5.11
,

R\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}t^{1/a_{1}},\overline{y}t^{1/a_{2}}]^{\operatorname{Int}},

which yields,by Lemma
3.1.21
, that
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}(R)\geq a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x})
, resulting in equality.

(3) If
\overline{x}
represents a partial maximal contact on
U
, then for any point
p\in U
, either
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}({\mathcal{A}})<a_{1}
or
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}({\mathcal{A}})=a_{1}
. According to Lemma
3.6.17
, in the latter case,
\overline{x}
can be extended to a maximal contact
\overline{x^{\prime}}
. Thus, we obtain the following:

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}^{1}(R)=a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x^{\prime}})\leq a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x}).

∎

Corollary 3.6.20
.

Let
\overline{x}
be a maximal contact of
R
at
p
.
Let

{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{\operatorname{int}}

be a maximal
R
-admissible center at a point
p\in X
. Then upon the change of the coordinate representation of
{\mathcal{A}}
,
\overline{x}_{1}=\overline{x}
is the maximal contact of
R
at
p
.

Proof.

According to Lemma
3.6.17
,
\overline{x}_{1}
contains
\overline{x}
after the coordinate change. Furthermore, based on Definition
3.6.18
and Lemma
3.6.19
(1), we have

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}^{1}(R)=a_{1}({\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x}_{1}))=a_{1}({\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x})).

This implies the equality
\overline{x}_{1}=\overline{x}
.
∎

3.6.21.
Support of the invariant

Let

{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1}):=\{p\in X\mid{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}(R)=\overline{b}_{1}\}

The following lemma highlights a fundamental property of maximal contact within our context:

Lemma 3.6.22
.

Let
\overline{x}
be a partial maximal contact of
(R,a_{1})
on
U
, and set
b_{1}:=a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x})
. Then we have

{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})\subseteq V(\overline{x}).

Proof.

Let’s denote
\overline{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},x_{r+1},\ldots,x_{s})
, where
x_{i}
are free on
U
for
1\leq i\leq r
and divisorial for
r+1\leq i\leq s
. Consider
q\in U\smallsetminus V(x_{i})
, where
x_{i}\in\overline{x}
is a free coordinate.

In this case, we have
x_{i}\in T^{1/a_{1}}(R)={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
, and
{\mathcal{D}}^{a_{1}b_{j}-1}(f_{j})
is invertible for some generator
f_{j}t^{b_{j}}\in R_{b_{j}}
. It follows that

{\operatorname{ord}}_{q}(f_{j}t^{b_{j}})\leq(a_{1}b_{j}-1)/b_{j}<a_{1},

which implies
{\operatorname{ord}}_{q}(R)<a_{1}
. Therefore, we have
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{q}(R)<\overline{b}_{1}
.

Consider
q\in V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})\cap U\smallsetminus V(x_{i})
, where
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}
are free coordinates, and
x_{i}
with
i>r
is a divisorial coordinate on
U
. Additionally, we have
D_{x_{i}}(T^{1/a_{1}}(R))={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
, which implies that
x_{i}
is a free coordinate at
q
since
q\not\in V(x_{i})
. Furthermore, for a certain local parameter
u\in T^{1/a_{1}}(R)
, we have
D_{x_{i}}(u)
invertible. Consequently,
u
is linearly independent from
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}
as well as from the other divisorial coordinates at
q
.

As a result, in a partial maximal contact
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},u)
of
R
at
q
, there are at least
r+1
free coordinates. According to Lemma
3.6.19
(3), this implies that

{\operatorname{inv}}_{q}^{1}(R)\leq a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}_{1}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},u)<\overline{b}_{1}=a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x}).

In this context, it is worth noting that the
(r+1)
-th component of

a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}_{1}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},u)
is
a_{1}
, while for
\overline{b}_{1}=a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x})
, it is
a_{1+}
.

∎

Example 3.6.23
.

Consider the ideal
{\mathcal{I}}=(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2})\subset K[x_{1},x_{2}]
, where
x_{1}
is a free coordinate and
x_{2}
is a divisorial coordinate.
Then

T^{1}({\mathcal{I}}t)=(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2})t

At the point
0
,
(x_{2})
represents a maximal contact, while on the scheme
X=\operatorname{Spec}(K[x_{1},x_{2}])
,
(x_{2})
corresponds to a partial maximal contact. Thus, we obtain the following:

{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{0}({\mathcal{I}}t)={\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(x_{2})=(1_{+})\quad\mbox{and}

{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}({\mathcal{I}}t,(1_{+}))\subseteq V(x_{2}).

When calculating the invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}t)
away from the origin, such as at
p\in V({\mathcal{I}})\smallsetminus{0}
, the coordinate
x_{1}^{\prime}:=x_{2}+x_{1}^{2}
is free. Therefore,
(x^{\prime}_{1},\in T^{2}({\mathcal{I}}t))
represents a maximal contact at
p
, which yields the following:

{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}t)={\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(x^{\prime}_{1})=(1)<(1_{+}).

Consequently, the invariant drops in a neighborhood of
0
.

This example illustrates the concept of assigning heavier weights:
1_{+}>1
, to the divisorial coordinates.

Lemma 3.6.24
.

Let
\overline{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})
be a partial maximal contact for
(R,a_{1})
on an open subset
U
. Then

(1)

{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})\subseteq V({T}^{\leq 1/a_{1}}(R))\cap V(\overline{x}).

(2)

\overline{x}
is a partial maximal contact at all points
q\in V({T}^{\leq 1/a_{1}}(R))\cap V(\overline{x})
for which
{\operatorname{ord}}_{q}(R)=a_{1}
.

(3)

{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})=V({T}^{\leq 1/a_{1}}(R))\cap V(\overline{x})\cap V(D_{x_{r+1},\ldots,x_{k}}(T^{1/a_{1}}R)))
is closed on
U
.

(4)

\overline{x}
is a maximal contact for the points in
{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})
.

Proof.

(1) and (2) Based on Lemmas
3.6.22
and
3.6.9
, we have

{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})\subset V({T}^{\leq 1/a_{1}}(R))\cap V(\overline{x}).

Furthermore, according to Lemma
3.6.14
, for points
q\in V({T}^{\leq 1/a_{1}}(R))\cap V(\overline{x})
, we have
{\operatorname{ord}}_{q}(R)\leq a_{1}
, and by Definition
3.6.11
,
\overline{x}
represents a partial maximal contact at
q
as long as
{\operatorname{ord}}_{q}(R)=a_{1}
.

(3) and (4). Let’s assume that
D_{x_{j}}({T}^{1/a_{1}}(R))={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
at
q\in V({T}^{\leq 1/a_{1}}(R))\cap V(\overline{x})
for some
j>r
.

If
x_{j}
is a divisorial coordinate, then
(\overline{x},x_{j})
represents a partial maximal contact at
q
, and according to Lemma
3.6.19
, we have
{\operatorname{inv}}_{q}(R)\leq a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x},x_{j})<\overline{b}_{1}
.

If
x_{j}
is a free coordinate at
q
, there exists a free local parameter
u\in{T}^{1/a_{1}}(R)
such that
D_{x_{j}}(u)
is invertible. In this case,
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},u)
represents a partial maximal contact at
q
, and we have
{\operatorname{inv}}_{q}(R)\leq a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x},x_{j})<\overline{b}_{1}
.

Conversely, if
q\in V({T}^{\leq 1/a_{1}}(R))\cap V(\overline{x})\cap V(D_{x_{r+1},\ldots,x_{k}}(T^{1/a_{1}}R))
, then the condition (3) of Definition
3.6.11
is satisfied, and
\overline{x}
represents a maximal contact at
q
. According to Lemma
3.6.19
, we have
{\operatorname{inv}}_{q}^{1}(R)=a_{1}({\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x}))
.

∎

3.6.25.
Maximal contact for nested ideals

We can directly extend the definition of maximal contact to nested Rees algebras.
Consider a coordinate system
\overline{x}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}_{n}
of local parameters that is compatible with
E
.
Let

R={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(x_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}t^{1/a_{k}})^{\operatorname{Int}},f_{j}t^{b_{j}}]_{j=1,\ldots,s}

be a Rees algebra nested at

H_{k}:=V(\overline{x}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k})
, with the nested order
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R_{|H_{k}})=a_{k+1}
.
The
nested cotangent ideal

T^{1/a_{k+1}}(R)
of
R
can be defined as follows:

T_{H_{K}}^{1/a_{k+1}}(R):=\sum_{|\alpha|=b_{j}a_{k+1}-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}{\mathcal{D}}_{\overline{x}_{k+1},\ldots,\overline{x}_{n}}^{\alpha}(f_{j})

in
t^{1/a_{k+1}}
- gradation. Its restriction
T_{H_{K}}^{1/a_{k+1}}(R)_{|H_{k}}
defines the cotangent ideal of
R_{|H_{k}}
on
H_{k}
. Consequently we define
a
nested maximal contact
for
R
at
H_{i}
given by a partial system of coordinates

\overline{x}_{k+1}=(x_{k+1,1},\ldots,x_{k+1,s},x_{k+1,s+1},\ldots,x_{k+1,r}))

on
X
, with free coordinates
x_{k+1,1},\ldots,x_{k+1,s}\in T_{H_{K}}^{a_{k+1}}(R)
, and divisorial

x_{k+1,s+1},\ldots,x_{k+1,r}
such that its restriction
\overline{x}_{k+1|H_{k}}
is a maximal contact for
R_{|H_{k}}
.

Corollary 3.6.26
.

Let

R={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(x_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}t^{1/a_{k}})^{\operatorname{Int}},f_{j}t^{b_{j}}]_{j=1,\ldots,s}

be a Rees algebra nested at

H_{k}:=V(\overline{x}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k})
, and assume that

{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{n}t^{1/a_{n}}]^{\operatorname{int}},

with
n\geq k
, is a maximal
R
- admissible center at
p\in X
so that

R\subset{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{\operatorname{Int}}

Then the nested order
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R_{|H_{k}})=a_{k+1}
.
Let
\overline{x}
be a nested maximal contact of
R
at
p
and
H_{k}
.
Then upon the change of the coordinate representation of
{\mathcal{A}}
,
\overline{x}_{k+1}=\overline{x}
is the maximal contact of
R
at
p
.

Proof.

Given that
R
is nested at
H_{k}
, it can be expressed in the split form in
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}
as:

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}\cdot R=\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}[(x_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}t^{1/a_{k}})^{\operatorname{Int}},R_{|H_{k}}]

Consequently,

{\mathcal{A}}_{|H_{k}}={\mathcal{O}}_{H}[\overline{x}_{k+1|H_{k}}t^{1/a{k+1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{n|H_{k}}t^{1/a_{n}}]^{\operatorname{int}}

serves as a maximal admissible center for
R_{|H_{k}}
. By applying Lemma
3.1.24
, we can observe that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R_{|H_{k}})=a_{k}
. According to Lemma
3.1.29
, the free coordinates in
\overline{x}
are a part of
\overline{x}_{k+1}
after a coordinate change. Moreover, since
\overline{x}_{|H_{k}}
represents a maximal contact for
R_{|H_{k}}
, we can deduce from Lemma
3.6.16
(4) that
\overline{x}_{k+1|H_{k}}
and
\overline{x}_{|H_{k}}
share the same divisorial coordinates. Consequently, we obtain the equality
\overline{x}_{k+1}=\overline{x}
.

∎

3.7.
Effective algorithm

3.7.1.
The algorithm

Consider an arbitrary Rees algebra
R
on a smooth variety
X
. In particular case, if an ideal
{\mathcal{I}}
is given we consider the Rees algebra
R={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[{\mathcal{I}}t]
.
We shall construct a maximal
{\mathcal{A}}
-admissible center
{\mathcal{R}}
at a given point
p\in X
.
Assume, at first that the Rees center

{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(x_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{{\operatorname{int}}},

which is maximal admissible at the point
p\in X
for
{\mathcal{R}}
on
X
exists. We shall use
{\mathcal{A}}
only as the reference for our construction. All the operations are done for
R
without using or modifying the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
. We will however modify the presentation of
{\mathcal{A}}
so it will be compatible with the presentations of modifications of
R
.

The procedure is recursive and we put
R=R_{1}
. Assume that the center
{\mathcal{A}}
is maximal admissible
R
, so that

R=R_{1}\subseteq{\mathcal{A}}:={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{{\operatorname{Int}}}.

Using Lemma
3.1.24
, we see that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R_{1})=a_{1}.

Let
\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}\subseteq T^{1/a_{1}}(R_{1})

be a maximal contact at
p\in X
, and put
H_{1}:=V(\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1})
.

By Lemma
3.6.20
, one can change the presentation of the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
so that
\overline{x}_{1}=\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}
is a maximal contact.
This modification does not affect the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
on the right, but only changes its presentation.

Let
R_{2}:=C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/{a_{1}}}}(R_{1})
be the coefficient ideal with respect to the maximal contact
\overline{x}_{1}
.
By Lemma
3.5.7
,
R_{2}
is nested at
H_{1}:=V(\overline{x}_{1})
, and can be written in
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}
in the split form

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}R_{2}=\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/{a_{1}}})^{\operatorname{Int}},R_{2|H_{1}}]=\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/{a_{1}}})^{\operatorname{Int}},C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/{a_{1}}}}(R_{1})_{|H_{1}}]

Furthermore, according to Lemma
3.5.24
, the same center
{\mathcal{A}}
remains maximal admissible for
R_{2}=C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/{a_{1}}}}(R_{1})
.

This recursive procedure can be summarized as follows.
Let
R_{i}
, for
i\geq 2
, be the algebra nested at
H_{i-1}=V(\overline{x}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}_{i-1})
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}R_{i}=\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{i-1}t^{1/a_{i-1}})^{\operatorname{Int}},R_{i|H_{i-1}}]\subseteq

\displaystyle\subseteq\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}{\mathcal{A}}=\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}[\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{{\operatorname{Int}}},

where
{\mathcal{A}}
is a maximal admissible center for
R_{i}
.
According to Lemma
3.6.26
, we have
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R_{i})=a_{i}
.
We find a nested maximal contact
\overline{x}^{\prime}_{i}
for
R_{i}
at
H_{i-1}
and
p
.
Using Lemma
3.6.26
we can modify the coordinates
\overline{x}_{i},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}
in the presentation of
{\mathcal{A}}
and assume that the maximal contact
\overline{x^{\prime}}_{i}=\overline{x}_{i}
occurs in the presentation of
{\mathcal{A}}
.
Consequently, by Lemma
3.5.24
,

R_{i+1}:=C_{\overline{x}_{i}t^{1/a_{i}}}(R_{i})\subseteq{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{Int}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{\operatorname{Int}},

where
{\mathcal{A}}
on the right represents a maximal admissible center for
R_{i+1}
.
Furthermore, by Lemma
3.5.18
,
R_{i+1}
is nested at
H_{i}=V(\overline{x}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}_{i})
, and can be written in

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}
as

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}\cdot R_{i+1}=\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{i}t^{1/a_{i}})^{\operatorname{Int}},R_{i+1|H_{i}}]=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}[(\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{i}t^{1/a_{i}})^{\operatorname{Int}},C_{\overline{x}_{i+1}}(R_{i})_{|H_{i}}],

We continue the procedure until we reach
R_{k+1}
which is nested at
H_{k}=V(\overline{x}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k})
, such that
R_{k+1|H_{k}}=0
. Finally

R_{k+1}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{\operatorname{Int}}={\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{Int}},

with the extended Rees algebra

{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1/w_{R,A}},\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}],

where
w_{R,A}={\operatorname{lcm}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{r},w_{R}).

3.7.2.
Uniqueness

Throughout this process, we do not alter the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1}]^{\operatorname{int}}
. However, we do modify the coordinates within
{\mathcal{A}}
, which gradually transform into maximal contacts for the nested Rees algebra on the left.

This demonstrates that the procedure, which is independent of
{\mathcal{A}}
, results in a unique, predetermined center
{\mathcal{A}}
that is maximal admissible for
R
at
p
.

3.7.3.
Existence

Proposition 3.7.4
.

(see also
[
ATW17
, Theorem 5.3.1]
(in the language of
{\mathbb{Q}}
-ideals)), ). For any Rees algebra
R
on a smooth variety
X
over
K
with a SNC divisor
E
, and for any point
p\in X
there exists a uniquely determined Rees center

{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1}]^{\operatorname{int}}

which is a maximal admissible for
R
at
p
.

Moreover
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{Int}}=R_{k+1}
is obtained by the recursive procedure
R_{1}=R
, and
R_{i+1}=C_{\overline{x}_{i}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R_{i})
independent of
{\mathcal{A}}
, where

(1)

R_{i}
is nested at
H_{i-1}:=V(\overline{x}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}_{i-1})
, for
i-1=1,\ldots,k
, and
H_{0}:=X
.

(2)

a_{i}={\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R_{i|H_{i-1}})

(3)

\overline{x}_{i}
is a maximal contact for
R_{i|H_{i-1}}
.

(4)

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)=(a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x}_{1}),\ldots,a_{k}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x}_{k}))
.

Proof.

The construction does not rely on the Rees center
{\mathcal{A}}
on the right side of the admissibility condition at any step. Moreover, the inductive process leads to a Rees center
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
that is admissible for
R
at
p
. We obtain a sequence of nested Rees algebras
R_{i}
at
H_{i-1}=V(\overline{x}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}_{i-1})
such that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R_{i|H_{i-1}})=a_{i}
and
\overline{x}_{i}
is a maximal contact for
R_{i|H_{i-1}}
. The final Rees center
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}=R_{k+1}^{\operatorname{int}}
is an admissible center for
R
.

Now, consider another admissible center

{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}t^{1/a^{\prime}_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}^{\prime}_{k}t^{1/a^{\prime}_{k}}]^{{\operatorname{int}}},

for
R
at
p
.
Suppose
{\operatorname{inv}}({\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime})\geq{\operatorname{inv}}({\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
. We run the recursive procedure using
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime}
on the right side. By Lemma
3.1.24
, we conclude that
a_{1}=a^{\prime\prime}_{1}
. Furthermore, according to Lemma
3.6.17
(2), after a coordinate change,
\overline{x}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
contains a maximal contact
\overline{x}_{1}
such that
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(\overline{x}_{1})\geq{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(\overline{x}^{\prime\prime}_{1})
. Since
{\operatorname{inv}}({\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime})\geq{\operatorname{inv}}({\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
, it follows that
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(\overline{x}^{\prime\prime}_{1})\geq{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(\overline{x}_{1})
. Therefore, we have
\overline{x}^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\overline{x}_{1}
. We continue this process step by step, demonstrating that
a_{i}=a_{i}^{\prime\prime}
and
\overline{x}^{\prime\prime}_{i}=\overline{x}_{i}
after a possible change in the coordinate representation of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
. This shows that
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}=R_{k+1}^{\operatorname{int}}
is the maximal admissible center for
{\mathcal{I}}
at
p
.
∎

3.7.5.
The inductive principle

[
ATW19
]
,

Let
R
be a Rees algebra of order
a_{1}
at
p
with a maximal contact
\overline{x}_{1}
, and let
H_{1}:=V(\overline{x}_{1})
. By Lemma
3.5.11
, a center
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{\operatorname{int}}
is maximal admissible for
R
if and only if
{\mathcal{A}}_{|H_{1}}={\mathcal{O}}_{H_{1}}[\overline{x}_{2|H_{1}}t^{1/a_{2}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k|H_{1}}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{{\operatorname{int}}}
is maximal admissible for
C_{\overline{x}_{1}(R)|H_{1}}
.

This leads to the following inductive formula, as stated in
[
ATW19
]
:

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)=({\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}(R),{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(C_{\overline{x}_{1}}(R)_{|H_{1}})),

3.7.6.
Semicontinuity of canonical invariant. Local admissibility

The inductive principle above ensures the semicontinuity of the invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}
, as in
[
ATW19
]
. We proceed with an induction on
n=\dim(X)
and assume that the function
p\mapsto{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)
is upper semicontinuous for any Rees algebra
R^{\prime}
on any smooth variety
H
of dimension
n-1
. Let
R
be a Rees algebra on a smooth
X
of dimension
n
.

The condition
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)\geq(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})
implies either
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}(R)>\overline{b}_{1}
, or
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}(R)=\overline{b}_{1}
and
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(C_{\overline{x}_{1}|H_{1}}(R))\geq(\overline{b}_{2},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})
. This description corresponds to a closed subset, which follows from the semicontinuity of
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}
as established in Lemma
3.6.24
, and with the inductive assumption.

3.7.7.
Duality of Rees centers

The Rees centers
\mathcal{A}
possess a dual interpretation: they serve as admissible centers with a dummy variable
t
, and they also represent the extended algebras of the full cobordant blow-ups, where
t^{-1}
serves as the introduced coordinate on
B
. These two concepts are intimately connected and can be described by identical formulas, up to rescaling. In order to prevent any confusion, we will introduce a distinct variable
t_{B}
specifically for the algebras on
B
, where the differentiation between
t
and
t_{B}
becomes necessary.

3.7.8.
Controlled transforms of ideals

[
ATW19
]
,
Let
{\mathcal{I}}
be an ideal on regular
X
. One writes
{\mathcal{I}}
-admissibility condition as:

{\mathcal{I}}t\subset{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1/w_{A}},\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]

By employing the variable
t_{B}
and applying rescaling, we can rewrite this inclusion as follows:

{\mathcal{I}}\cdot t_{B}^{w_{A}}\,\,\subset\,\,{\mathcal{O}}_{B}\,=\,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t_{B}^{-1},\overline{x}_{1}t_{B}^{w_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t_{B}^{w_{k}}]={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t_{B}^{-1},\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}^{\prime}_{k}],

where
x_{i}^{\prime}:=x_{i}t^{w_{i}}
.

According to Lemma
2.5.1
, the exceptional divisor on
B_{+}
can be expressed as
t_{B}^{-1}
, which serves as a local parameter on
B
. Using this information, we can deduce that the full transform
\mathcal{O}_{B}\cdot I
is divisible by
t_{B}^{-w_{A}}
since
t^{w_{A}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{B}\cdot\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{O}_{B}\cdot t_{B}^{w_{A}}\cdot\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{O}_{B}
.

Now, we introduce the concept of the
controlled transform
of the ideal
\mathcal{I}
, which is defined as:

\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{I}}):={\mathcal{O}}_{B}\cdot t_{B}^{w_{A}}\cdot{\mathcal{I}}\subset B.

3.7.9.
Strict transform of ideals

Recall that

B_{-}=B\smallsetminus V(t^{-1})=X\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to X

is trivial over
X
.
By
the strict transform

\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}})
of an ideal
{\mathcal{I}}
on
X
under a full cobordant blow-up
\sigma:B\to X
of
{{\mathcal{A}}}
on
X
we mean the schematic closure of
({\mathcal{O}}_{B}\cdot{\mathcal{I}}_{X})_{|B_{-}}={\mathcal{O}}_{B_{-}}\cdot{\mathcal{I}}_{X}
.
Thus

\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}}):=\{t^{a}f\in{\mathcal{O}}_{B}\mid\quad f\,\in\,{\mathcal{O}}_{B}\cdot{\mathcal{I}}_{Y},\quad a\geq 0\}.

Consequently, the strict transform of a closed subscheme
Y
of
X
is the schematic closure
Y^{s}
of
Y\times G_{m}\subset B_{-}=X\times G_{m}
in
B
. It is defined by

{\mathcal{I}}_{Y^{s}}=\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y})
.
This implies that
\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{I}})\subseteq\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}})
, and thus
{\operatorname{inv}}(\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{I}}))\geq{\operatorname{inv}}(\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}}))
for any
p\in B
.

Remark 3.7.10
.

Observe that for the cobordant blow-up
\sigma_{+}:B_{+}\to X
the induced strict transform coincides with the standard definition
\sigma_{+}^{s}({\mathcal{I}})
as the schematic closure on
B_{+}
of

{\mathcal{O}}_{\sigma_{+}^{-1}(X\smallsetminus V({\mathcal{J}}))}\cdot{\mathcal{I}}_{|X\smallsetminus V({\mathcal{J}})}=({\mathcal{O}}_{B}\cdot{\mathcal{I}})_{|{B_{+}\cap B_{-}}}.

3.7.11.
Controlled transforms of Rees algebras and double gradation

The relationship between admissibility and the controlled transforms of ideals can be described using the concept of double gradation. Let
\mathcal{I}
correspond to the gradation
\mathcal{I}t
in the Rees algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{I}}=\mathcal{O}_{X}[\mathcal{I}t]
. We can assign a double gradation
\mathcal{O}_{B}\cdot\mathcal{I}\cdot t^{w_{A}}_{B}t
in the Rees algebra
\mathcal{O}_{B}[\mathcal{I}\cdot t^{w_{A}}_{B}t]
on
B
, which can be interpreted as the controlled transform denoted by

\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{I}}t):=({\mathcal{O}}_{B}\cdot t_{B}^{w}\cdot{\mathcal{I}})t\subset\mathcal{O}_{B}[\mathcal{I}\cdot t^{w_{A}}_{B}t]

under the gradation
t
. One can simply write it as

\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{I}}):=({\mathcal{O}}_{B}\cdot t_{B}^{w}\cdot{\mathcal{I}})\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{B}

In a more general setting, suppose we have a Rees algebra
R=\bigoplus R_{a}t^{a}
, and
\mathcal{A}
is an
R
-admissible center. Then, we have the inclusion

R\subset{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1/w},\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}],

where
w
is a multiple of
w_{R,A}
. By rescaling
\mathcal{A}
with
t_{B}\mapsto t_{B}^{w}
and defining
w_{i}:=w/a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we obtain the cobordant blow-up of
\mathcal{A}^{\text{ext}}
:

B=\operatorname{Spec}([t_{B}^{-1},\overline{x}_{1}t_{B}^{w_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t_{B}^{w_{k}}]=\operatorname{Spec}{\mathcal{O}}_{B}[t_{B}^{-1},\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}^{\prime}_{k}],

where
x_{i}^{\prime}:=x_{i}t_{B}^{w_{i}}
.
By combining the gradations on both
t
and
t^{w}_{B}
, we can express the admissibility of Rees algebras on
B
as follows:

\displaystyle\bigoplus R_{a}t_{B}^{a\cdot w}t^{a}\,\,\subset\,\,{\mathcal{O}}_{B}[t_{B}^{-1}t^{-1/w},\overline{x}_{1}t_{B}^{w_{1}}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t_{B}^{w_{k}}t^{1/a_{k}}]\,\,\subset

\displaystyle\subset\,\,{\mathcal{O}}_{B}[t^{-1/w},\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}^{\prime}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}].

Hence, the Rees algebra on
B
can be denoted as:

\sigma^{c}(R):=\bigoplus({\mathcal{O}}_{B}\cdot R_{a}\cdot t_{B}^{a\cdot w})t^{a},

and it will be referred to as the
controlled transform of
R
.

3.7.12.
Cobordant blow-ups and admissibility

Consequently, based on the previous discussion, we can conclude that:

Lemma 3.7.13
.

If
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1/w},\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{\operatorname{int}}
is admissible for
R
, and

\sigma:B=\operatorname{Spec}_{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{B}[t_{B}^{-1},\overline{x}_{1}t_{B}^{w_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t_{B}^{w_{k}}])
is the full cobordant blow-up of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}
then the Rees center on
B
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{B}:=\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}):={\mathcal{O}}_{B}[t^{-1/w},\overline{x}_{1}t_{B}^{w_{1}}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t_{B}^{w_{k}}t^{1/a_{k}}]^{\operatorname{int}}=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{B}[t^{-1/w},\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}^{\prime}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]

is admissible for
\sigma^{c}(R)=\bigoplus{\mathcal{O}}_{B}R_{a}t_{B}^{wa}t^{a}
. ∎

3.7.14.
Derivations on cobordant blow-up

[
ATW19
]
,

Consider be the full cobordant blow-up

B=\operatorname{Spec}_{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t_{B}^{-1},x_{1}t_{B}^{w_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}t_{B}^{w_{k}}]\to X

of a center
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1/w},x_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]
.
The sheaf
\mathcal{D}_{X}
of derivations on
X
is a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module that is locally generated by the derivations
D_{x_{i}}
.
Using chain rule we can write derivations
D_{x_{i}^{\prime}}
on
B
as

D_{x_{i}^{\prime}}=t_{B}^{-w_{i}}D_{x_{i}}
, and
D_{x_{j}^{\prime}}=D_{x_{j}}
.

This above formula can be extended using a double gradation principle:
To obtain the controlled transform, we associate the derivations
t^{-1/a_{i}}D_{x_{i}}
in the graded form with a double gradation with respect to
t
and
t_{B}^{w}
.

\sigma^{c}(t^{-1/a_{i}}D_{x_{i}})=t^{-1/a_{i}}t_{B}^{-w_{i}}D_{x_{i}}=t^{-1/a_{i}}D_{x_{j}^{\prime}}

In a similar manner, we define the ”controlled transform” of
t^{-1/a_{1}}\mathcal{D}_{X}
to be the subsheaf

\sigma^{c}(t^{-1/a_{1}}{\mathcal{D}}_{X}):=t^{-1/a_{1}}{\mathcal{O}}_{B}t_{B}^{-w_{1}}{\mathcal{D}}_{X}\subseteq t^{-1/a_{1}}{\mathcal{D}}_{B}

of
the sheaf
{\mathcal{D}}_{B}
of the derivations on
B
in gradation
t^{-1/a_{1}}
. Note that the sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{B}t_{B}^{-w_{1}}\cdot{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
is generated
by
t_{B}^{-(w_{1}-w_{i})}t_{B}^{-w_{i}}D_{x_{i}}=t_{B}^{-(w_{1}-w_{i})}D_{x^{\prime}_{i}}
for
i=1,\ldots,k
and
t_{B}^{-w_{1}}D_{x_{i}}=t_{B}^{-w_{1}}D_{x^{\prime}_{i}}
for
i=k+1,\ldots,n
.

3.7.15.
The order of the controlled transforms

Lemma 3.7.16
.

[
ATW19
]
, Let

\sigma:B\to X
be a cobordant blow-up of
R
-admissible center
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{1/w},\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]
, where
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R)\leq a_{1}
for
p\in X
. Then
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p^{\prime}}(\sigma^{c}(R))\leq a_{1}
for
p^{\prime}\in B
.

Proof.

Write
R={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[f_{j}t^{b_{j}}]_{j=1,\ldots,s}
. If
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(R)=a_{1}
then
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(f_{j}t^{b_{j}})=b_{j}a_{1}
for some
j
.
Thus there exists
\alpha
, with
|\alpha|=b_{j}a_{1}
, such that
{\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\alpha}}f_{j}
is invertible. Consequently

\displaystyle 1\sim

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\alpha}}f_{j}=(t_{B}^{-b_{j}w}t^{-b_{j}}{\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\alpha}})(f_{j}t_{B}^{b_{j}w}t^{b_{j}})\in\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{b_{j}a_{1}}t^{-b_{j}})(\sigma^{c}(f_{j}t^{b_{j}}))

\displaystyle\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}_{B}^{b_{j}a_{1}}t^{-b_{j}}(\sigma^{c}(R_{b_{j}})t^{b_{j}})={\mathcal{D}}_{B}^{b_{j}a_{1}}(\sigma^{c}(R_{b_{j}}))={\mathcal{O}}_{B},

which shows that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}\sigma^{c}(R)\leq a_{1}
.
∎

3.7.17.
Controlled transforms of cotangent ideal

Similarly we have

Lemma 3.7.18
.

[
ATW19
]
, Let
\sigma:B\to X
be a cobordant blow-up of
R={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[f_{j}t^{b_{j}}]
-admissible center
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{1/w},\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]
. If
T^{1/a_{1}}(R)
is the cotangent ideal for
R

then

\sigma^{c}(T^{1/a_{1}}(R)t^{1/a_{1}})\subseteq(T^{1/a_{1}}(\sigma^{c}(R))t^{1/a_{1}}.

Proof.

Let
f_{j}t^{b_{j}}\in R_{b_{j}}
, and
|\alpha|=b_{j}a_{1}-1
. Then

\displaystyle({\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\alpha}}f_{j})t^{1/a_{1}}t_{B}^{w_{1}}=\sigma^{c}(({\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\alpha}}f_{j})t^{1/a_{1}})=\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{D}}^{\alpha}_{\overline{x}t^{1/a_{1}}}f_{j}t^{b_{j}})=

\displaystyle=\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{D}}^{\alpha}_{\overline{x}t^{1/a_{1}}})(\sigma^{c}(f_{j}t^{b_{j}})\in{\mathcal{D}}_{B}^{|\alpha|}t^{-|\alpha|/a_{1}}(\sigma^{c}(R_{b_{j}})t^{b_{j}})=

\displaystyle={\mathcal{D}}_{B}^{|\alpha|}(\sigma^{c}(R_{b_{j}}))t^{1/a_{1}}\subseteq(T^{1/a_{1}}(\sigma^{c}(R))t^{1/a_{1}}.

∎

3.7.19.
Controlled transforms of a partial maximal contact

Lemma 3.7.20
.

[
ATW19
]
, Let

\sigma:B\to X
be a cobordant blow-up of
R={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[f_{j}t^{b_{j}}]
-admissible center
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{1/w},\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]
. Assume that
\overline{x}_{1}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})
is a partial maximal contact for
(R,a_{1})
on an open affine subset
U

then
\sigma^{c}(\overline{x}_{1})
is a partial maximal contact for
\sigma^{c}(R)
on
\sigma^{-1}(U)
.

Proof.

\sigma^{c}(\overline{x}_{1})=(x_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{r})
is a local system of coordinates.
So if
x_{i}\in T^{1/a_{1}}(R)\cap\overline{x}_{1}
is free then
x_{i}^{\prime}=\sigma^{c}(x_{i})\in T^{1/a_{1}}(\sigma^{c}(R))\cap\sigma^{c}(\overline{x}_{1})
is free. Thus condition (1) of Definition
3.6.11
is satisfied.

If
x_{i}\in\overline{x}_{1}
is divisorial, then using Lemma
3.7.18
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{B}={\mathcal{O}}_{B}\cdot D_{x_{i}}(T^{1/a_{1}}(R))=\sigma^{c}(D_{x_{i}t^{1/a_{1}}}(T^{1/a_{1}}(R)t^{1/a_{1}})=

\displaystyle=\sigma^{c}(D_{x_{i}t^{1/a_{1}}})\sigma^{c}(T^{1/a_{1}}(R)t^{1/a_{1}})=

\displaystyle=t^{-1/a_{1}}D_{x^{\prime}_{i}}\sigma^{c}((T^{1/a_{1}}(R)t^{1/a_{1}})\subset D_{x^{\prime}_{i}}(T^{1/a_{1}}(\sigma^{c}(R))

and thus condition (2) of Lemma
3.6.11
is also satisfied.

∎

3.7.21.
Controlled transform of the coefficient ideal

Lemma 3.7.22
.

(see also
[
ATW19
]
) Let

\sigma:B\to X
be a cobordant blow-up of
R={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[f_{j}t^{b_{j}}]
-admissible algebra
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]
then
\sigma^{c}(R)={\mathcal{O}}_{B}[\sigma^{c}(f_{j}t^{b_{j}})]
, and we have commutativity:

\sigma^{c}(C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R))=C_{\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(\sigma^{c}(R)).

Proof.

The assertion follows from the of the commutativity of the controlled transforms with derivations:

\sigma^{c}(D^{\alpha}_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(f_{j}t^{b_{j}}))=\sigma^{c}(D^{\alpha}_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}})(\sigma^{c}(f_{j}t^{b_{j}}))=D^{\alpha}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(\sigma^{c}(f_{j}t^{b_{j}}))

in
t^{|\alpha|}
-gradation for
|\alpha|<b_{j}a_{1}
.
∎

3.7.23.
Restriction of cobordant blow-up to a maximal contact

Lemma 3.7.24
.

[
ATW19
]
, If
{\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}
is admissible for
R
then
{\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}_{|H}
is admissible for
R_{|H}
and
\sigma^{c}(R)_{|H}=\sigma_{H}^{c}(R_{|H})
, where
H:=V(\overline{x}_{1})
.

The restriction of the blow-up
\sigma_{X}:B\to X
of
{\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}
to the strict transform
H_{B}=V(\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1})
of
H=V(\overline{x}_{1})
is the cobordant blow-up
\sigma_{H}:H_{B}\to H
of the restriction
{\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}_{|H}
. ∎

3.7.25.
The centers with maximal invariant

Lemma 3.7.26
.

(see also
[
ATW19
]
) Let
R
be a Rees algebra, and
{\mathcal{A}}
be a maximal admissible center for
R
at a point
p\in X
. Then there exists an open neighborhood
U
of
p
such that

{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{U}(R)={\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)={\operatorname{inv}}({\mathcal{A}})

on
U
is attained at
V({\mathcal{A}})
.

Proof.

Let
\overline{x}_{1}
be a maximal contact at
p
for
R
. Then, by Lemma
3.6.12
, it is a partial maximal contact on an open subset
U
of
p
. Thus by Lemma
3.6.19
(3),

{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R)\leq\overline{b}_{1}:=a_{1}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x}_{1}).

In addition, by Lemma
3.6.24
, the maximum
\max{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R)=\overline{b}_{1}
of the invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{q}(R)
, where
q\in X
, is attained at the closed subset
{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})\subset H_{1}=V(\overline{x}_{1})
, and
\overline{x}_{1}
is a maximal contact along
{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})
. Furthermore for the points in
{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})
, we have

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)=({\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}(R),{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R)_{|H_{1}}),

Moreover
{\mathcal{A}}_{|H_{1}}
is maximal admissible for
R
at
p
.
It suffices to use induction on dimension to find an open neighborhood
U
of
p
such that
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R)_{|H_{1}})
attains its maximal on
V({\mathcal{A}}_{|H_{1}})=V({\mathcal{A}})\subset H
.

∎

Proposition 3.7.27
.

,
Let
R=\bigoplus R_{a}
be a Rees algebra on a smooth variety
X
over a field
K
such that
R_{a}\neq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
for any
a\in A
.
There exists a unique center
{\mathcal{A}}(R)={\mathcal{A}}
such that

(1)

The maximum
{\operatorname{maxinv}}(R)=(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})
of the invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)
, where
p\in X
, is attained at
V({\mathcal{A}})
.

(2)

{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]
is a maximal admissible center for
R
, with
{\operatorname{inv}}({\mathcal{A}})=(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})
, where
\overline{b_{i}}=a_{i}{\operatorname{inv}}(\overline{x}_{i})
.

Proof.

Lemma
3.7.26
implies that
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)
is upper semicontinuous, and admits finitely many values. Consider the closed point set

S:={\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}(R,(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})):=\{p\in X\mid{\operatorname{inv}}(R)=(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})\},

where
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)
, where
p\in X
, attains its maximal value
{\operatorname{maxinv}}(R)=(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})
. Then for any point
p\in S
let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a maximal admissible center for
R
at
p
. Then

V({\mathcal{A}})={\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}(R,(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k}))

locally around
p
. Moreover,
{\mathcal{A}}
is a unique maximal admissible center for
R
at all points of
V({\mathcal{A}})
in a neighborhood of
p
. Thus
{\mathcal{A}}
glues to a unique maximal admissible center for
R
along
V({\mathcal{A}})={\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}(R,(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k}))
as desired.

∎

3.7.28.
Cobordant blow-ups of the centers with maximal invariant

Proposition 3.7.29
.

(see also
[
ATW19
]
) Let
R=\bigoplus R_{a}
be a Rees algebra on a smooth variety
X
over a field
K
such that
R_{a}\neq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
for any
a\in A
.
Let
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}(R)
be maximal admissible center for
R
at
V({\mathcal{A}})
, and
B\to X
be a full cobordant blow-up of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}
. Then in a neighborhood of
V({\mathcal{A}})
such that
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)
attains its maximum
{\operatorname{maxinv}}(\sigma^{c}(R))=(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})
at
V({\mathcal{A}})
we have

(1)

The maximum
{\operatorname{maxinv}}(\sigma^{c}(R))=(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})
is attained at

V(\sigma^{c}(A))
with the invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}(\sigma^{c}(R))
, and such that

\sigma^{c}(A^{{\operatorname{ext}}})={\mathcal{O}}_{B}[t^{-1/w},x^{\prime}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}]

is maximal admissible center for
\sigma^{c}(R)

(2)

{\operatorname{maxinv}}(\sigma^{c}(R))<(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})
on
B_{+}=B\smallsetminus V(\sigma^{c}(A))
.

Proof.

We shall use induction on dimension of
X
.
If
\dim(X)=0
then
{\mathcal{A}}=R={\mathcal{O}}_{X}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[0]
,
p=X
, and
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)=()
has no entries and thus corresponds to the infinite sequence of
\infty
.
Let
p\in V({\mathcal{A}})
, and set
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}({\mathcal{A}})=a_{1}
.

Consider a neighborhood
U
of
p
such that
\overline{x}_{1}
is a maximal contact on
U
at
p
, and a partial maximal contact on
U
. Then, by Lemma
3.6.24
,
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}
attains its maximum
\overline{b}_{1}
on
{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})\subset V(\overline{x}_{1})
. Moreover
\overline{x}_{1}
is a maximal contact along
{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})
.

Consequently
for the points
p\in{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})
we have

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)=({\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}(R),{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R)_{|H_{1}})).

By Lemma
3.7.20
, the controlled transform
\sigma^{c}(\overline{x}_{1})=\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}
is a partial maximal contact on
B_{U}=\sigma^{-1}(U)
for
\sigma^{c}(R)
. Then, by Lemma
3.6.24
,
{\operatorname{maxinv}}^{1}(\sigma^{c}(R))\leq\overline{b}_{1}
, and it attains its value
\overline{b}_{1}
on

{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(\sigma^{c}(R),\overline{b}_{1})\subseteq H^{\prime}_{1}=V(\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}),

and for the points
p^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{supp}}{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}(R,\overline{b}_{1})
we have that
\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}
is a maximal contact. Moreover, by Lemma
3.7.22
, and Section
3.7.5
we get

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p^{\prime}}(\sigma^{c}(R))=({\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p^{\prime}}(\sigma^{c}(R)),{\operatorname{inv}}_{p^{\prime}}(C_{\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(\sigma^{c}(R))_{|H^{\prime}_{1}}).

By Lemma
3.7.24
, the restriction of
B\to X
to
H^{\prime}_{1}=V_{B}(\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1})
is the cobordant blow-up
\sigma_{H^{\prime}_{1}}:H^{\prime}_{1}\to H_{1}
of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{|H_{1}}
.
By the inductive assumption on dimension the conditions (1), (2) of the Proposition are satisfied on
H_{1}
for
C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R)_{|H_{1}}
and for the cobordant blow-up
\sigma_{H^{\prime}_{1}}:H^{\prime}_{1}\to H_{1}
.
Moreover, by Lemma
3.5.11
, and the assumption,
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{|H_{1}}
is maximal admissible for
C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R)_{|H_{1}}
on
H_{1}
, associated with
{\operatorname{maxinv}}(C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R)_{|H_{1}})
.

Consequently, by the inductive argument
\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}})_{|H_{1}}={\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{B|H^{\prime}_{1}}
is maximal admissible for

\sigma_{H^{\prime}_{1}}^{c}(C_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R)_{|H_{1}})=C_{\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(\sigma^{c}(R))_{|H^{\prime}_{1}}.

By Lemma
3.5.11
and the fact that
\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}
is a partial maximal contact associated with
{\operatorname{maxinv}}^{1}(\sigma^{c}(R))=\overline{b}_{1}
we conclude that
{\mathcal{A}}_{B}^{\operatorname{ext}}
is maximal admissible for
\sigma^{c}(R)
.
By the inductive assumption,
the maximum

{\operatorname{maxinv}}(\sigma^{c}(C_{\overline{x^{\prime}}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R)_{|H_{1}}))=(\overline{b}_{2},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})

on
B
is attained at
V(\sigma_{H_{1}}^{c}({\mathcal{A}}_{|H_{1}})
. Thus

{\operatorname{maxinv}}(\sigma^{c}(R)_{|H^{\prime}_{1}})=\max(\overline{b}_{1},{\operatorname{inv}}(C_{\overline{x^{\prime}}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}(R)_{|H_{1}})=(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{k})

is attained at

V(\sigma_{H_{1}}^{c}({\mathcal{A}}_{|H_{1}})=V(\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{A}})_{|H^{\prime}_{1}})=V(\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{A}}))\subseteq H^{\prime}_{1},

and
\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{A}})
is a maximal admissible center for
\sigma^{c}(R)
associated with
{\operatorname{maxinv}}\sigma^{c}(R)
.
∎

3.7.30.
Resolution principle

(see also
[
ATW19
]
for (1), (2) and the second part of (4))
Summarizing the above, the resolution process consists of the following:

Given a rational Rees algebra on a smooth
X
with SNC divisor
E
over a field
K
of characteristic zero.

(1)

The invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)
on
X
is semicontinuous. (Section
3.7.6
)

(2)

It attains its maximum
{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{X}(R)
at a certain unique center
{\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}(R)
on
X
. (Proposition
3.7.27
)

(3)

The invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(\sigma^{c}(R))
on the full cobordant blow-up
B\to X
at
{\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}(R)
, attains its maximum

{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{X}(R)={\operatorname{maxinv}}_{B}(\sigma^{c}(R))

exactly at the center
\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}(R))
associated with the vertex
{\operatorname{Vert}}(B)=V(\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}))
of the full cobordant blow-up
B\to X
.(Proposition
3.7.29
(1))

(4)

The invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(.)
drops on the cobordant blow-up

B_{+}=B\smallsetminus V(\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}(R)))=B\smallsetminus{\operatorname{Vert}}(B)

after removing the vertex
{\operatorname{Vert}}(B)
, so that

{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{X}(R)>{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{B_{+}}(\sigma^{c}(R)).

(Proposition
3.7.29
(2))

3.8.
Properties of the invariant

3.8.1.
The invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}
at the smooth points

Assume that
Y
is a smooth subvariety of codimension
k
on a smooth variety
X
and is described at
p\in Y
by a partial set of free local parameters
Y=V(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k})
compatible with an SNC divisor
E
.
Then

{\mathcal{A}}:={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k})t]

is a maximal
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}
- admissible center at
p
, with

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}})=(1,\ldots,1),

with
k
entries equal
1
. Conversely, if

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y})=(1,\ldots,1)
is as above then there exists a partial system of free local parameters
u_{1},\ldots,u_{k}\in{\mathcal{I}}
compatible with
E
, such that

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}t]\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k})t]^{\operatorname{int}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k})t].

So
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k})
is smooth generated by free coordinates and compatible with
E
for
Y
having SNC with
E
at
p\in X
.

3.8.2.
Torus action

If
X
admits a torus action and
R
is
T
-stable, then the maximal admissible centers are canonical and unique and thus
T
-stable. One can run the algorithm in Section
3.7.1
using seminvariant maximal contacts and seminvariant derivations, with all intermediate Rees algebras
R_{i}
being
T
-stable. Thus one can choose inductively semiinvariant coordinates of the maximal admissible centers. These are the nested maximal contacts in the
T
-stable cotangent ideals
T^{1/a_{i}}(R_{i})
.

Moreover, if additionally
X
admits a geometric quotient
X/T
with all orbits of dimension
\dim(T)
, then the number of semiinvariant coordinates in a
T
-stable center at a given point cannot exceed
\dim(X/T)=\dim(X)-\dim(T)
, as it describes a smooth
T
-stable subvariety of
X
of dimension at least
\dim(T)
.
Consequently the set of values of
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}})
in the resolution process

X_{0}\leftarrow X_{1}\leftarrow X_{2}\leftarrow\ldots

is contained in
({\mathbb{Q}}_{+})_{\geq 0}^{k}
, where

k=\dim(X_{0})=\ldots=\dim(X_{i})-\dim({\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{i}).

3.8.3.
The descending chain condition

Lemma 3.8.4
.

The set
\Gamma\subset({\mathbb{Q}}_{+})_{\geq 0}^{k}
, of the possible values of
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)
of the
T
-stable Rees algebras on a smooth varieties
X
with action of torus
T
, and having a geometric quotient
X/T
of dimension
k
, which are generated by the gradations
R_{a^{i}}t^{a^{i}}
, for
i=1,\ldots,s
satisfies dcc.

Proof.

Donote by
a:={\operatorname{lcm}}(a^{1},\ldots,a^{s}))
.
We use induction on
k
. For
k=1
, the possible values of
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)={\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}(R)
are contained in
(1/a)\cdot{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
.

In general, we note that
{\mathcal{A}}
is
R
-admissible if and only if
R_{a}t^{a}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
. Thus we have

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)=1/a\cdot({\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R_{a}),

and the problem reduces to the ideals
{\mathcal{I}}=R_{a}
on
X
.

Now, for any ideal
{\mathcal{I}}
on
X
we have

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}})=({\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}),{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(\sigma_{H_{1}}^{c}({\mathcal{C}}_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[({\mathcal{I}}t)]_{|H_{1}})),

where the restricted coefficient ideal

C:={\mathcal{C}}_{\overline{x}_{1}t^{1/a_{1}}}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[({\mathcal{I}}t)]_{|H_{1}})=\bigoplus C_{a/a_{1}}\cdot t^{a/a_{1}}

is generated at gradations

C_{1/a_{1}}t^{1/a_{1}},\ldots,C_{(a_{1}-1)/a_{1}}t^{(a_{1}-1)/a_{1}}

on
H_{1}
with
\dim(H_{1}/T)<k
.
By the inductive assumption the set of values of
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(C)

satisfies dcc. Now since the set of values
{\operatorname{inv}}^{1}_{p}({\mathcal{I}})
satisfies dcc condition we conclude that the set of values
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}})
on
X
satisfies dcc.

Consequently the set of values of
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)
in a resolution sequence also satisfies dcc.
∎

3.8.5.
Functoriality of the invariant

[
ATW19
]

Lemma 3.8.6
.

The invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)
and the maximal admissible center
{\mathcal{A}}
are functorial under smooth morphisms, field extensions, and group actions.

The functoriality of
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R)
and the maximal admissible centers
{\mathcal{A}}
at
p
is a consequence of the functoriality of the resolution algorithm in Section
3.7.1
and functoriality of derivations, Rees algebras, coefficient ideals, and the maximal contacts.

3.9.
Final Conclusions

3.9.1.
Functorial Principalization

[
ATW19
]
(in a non-SNC setting),

Let
{\mathcal{I}}
be an ideal on
X
. We initiate the SNC resolution algorithm using cobordant blow-ups
\sigma_{i}:X_{i+1}\to X_{i}
at
the maximal
{\mathcal{I}}_{i}
-admissible centers
{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{I}}_{i})
on
X_{i}
, associated with the maximum
{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{X_{i}}({\mathcal{I}}_{i})
of
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}_{i})
on
X_{i}
.
They form a sequence

X=X_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{0}}}{{\leftarrow}}X_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{1}}}{{\leftarrow}}\ldots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{k-1}}}{{\leftarrow}}X_{k}=X^{\prime},

Here the ideal
{\mathcal{I}}_{0}:={\mathcal{I}}
, and
{\mathcal{I}}_{i+1}:=\sigma_{i}^{c}({\mathcal{I}}_{i})
is the controlled transform of
{\mathcal{I}}_{i}
.
Each such blow-up reduces
{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{X_{i}}({\mathcal{I}}_{i})
so that

{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{X_{i}}({\mathcal{I}}_{i})>{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{X_{i+1}}({\mathcal{I}}_{i+1}),

where
X_{i+1}
is the coborandant blow-ups
X_{i}
at the center
{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{I}}_{i})
.
By dcc property this is a finite process which continues until the maximum value

{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{X_{k}}(\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{I}}_{k}))=0.

In this situation, the controlled transform

\sigma^{c}({\mathcal{I}}):=\sigma_{k-1}^{c}({\mathcal{I}}_{i})\circ\ldots\circ\sigma_{1}^{c}({\mathcal{I}})={\mathcal{O}}_{X}

of
{\mathcal{I}}
becomes
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
, and the full transform
{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{\prime}}\cdot{\mathcal{I}}
is locally the product of divisorial components. Note that each cobordant blow-up
X_{i+1}\to X_{i}
inductively creates a scheme
X_{i+1}
with an action of
T_{i+1}=T_{i}\times G_{m}
. Since all constructions are canonical and functorial for smooth morphisms, the centers, the ideals
{\mathcal{I}}_{i}
and the exceptional divisors are automatically
T_{i}
-stable. This proves Theorem
1.11.2
.

3.9.2.
Embedded Desingularization

[
ATW19
]

In the process of embedded resolution of an irreducible subvariety
Y\subset X
of codimension
k
, we consider the principalization of
{\mathcal{I}}={\mathcal{I}}_{Y}
of
Y
on
X
. We execute the algorithm applying the cobordant blow-ups at maximal
{\mathcal{I}}
-admissible centers
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{I}})
. In this process, we use the strict transforms of the ideal
{\mathcal{I}}={\mathcal{I}}_{Y}
instead of the controlled transforms.
We stop the algorithm when the invariant

{\operatorname{maxinv}}_{X}({\mathcal{I}})=(1,\ldots,1),

with
k
entries equal
1
.
In such a case, the associated maximal admissible center with the invariant
{\operatorname{inv}}({\mathcal{A}})=(1,\ldots,1)
for the ideal
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y^{\prime}}
of the strict transform
Y^{\prime}
of
Y
coincides with
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[{\mathcal{I}}_{Y^{\prime}}t]
, and thus, by Section
3.8.1
, the strict transform
Y^{\prime}
of
Y
has SNC with the exceptional divisor, which is also SNC.

Alternatively one can use the controlled transforms of ideals instead of the strict transforms in the process without changing the strategy. The process is slower and some centers need not to be contained in the strict transform of
Y
.

Both procedures show the existence of the functorial embedded desingularization by the smooth cobordant blow-ups as in Theorem
1.11.4
. As a result, we obtain a smooth subvariety
Y^{\prime}
having SNC with an SNC exceptional divisor
E^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
, and with the torus action.

Each consecutive cobordant blow-up
X_{i+1}\to X_{i}
will create inductively a variety
X_{i+1}
with an action of
T_{i+1}=T_{i}\times G_{m}
. Since all the constructions are canonical and functorial for smooth morphisms, the centers and subvariety
Y_{i}\subset X_{i}
are automatically
T_{i}
-stable. On the level of the geometric quotients, we obtain the sequence of weighted blow-ups

X=X_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{0}}}{{\leftarrow}}X_{1}/T_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{1/T_{1}}}}{{\leftarrow}}\ldots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{k-1/T_{k-1}}}}{{\leftarrow}}X_{k}/T_{k}=X^{\prime}/T,

of varieties with quotient singularities such that the
subvariety
Y^{\prime}/T
of
X^{\prime}/T
admits quotient singularities. When considering stack-theoretic quotients
[X_{i}/T_{i}]
, we obtain smooth stacks with a smooth substack
[Y^{\prime}/T]\subset[X^{\prime}/T]
having SNC with the exceptional divisors. A similar process without exceptional SNC divisor was considered in
[
ATW19
]
.

3.9.3.
Nonembedded SNC Resolution

The nonembedded resolution is a consequnce of the embedded resolution using local embeddings and the functoriality properties. It will have SNC exceptional divisor.

We associate with a variety
Y
over
K
, initially without any divisors, a modified invariant
\widetilde{{\operatorname{inv}}}_{p}(Y)
in the following way. We locally embed
Y
into a smooth variety
X
. Any two such embeddings
Y\subset X_{1}
and
Y\subset X_{2}
into smooth varieties of the same dimension are étale equivalent. If
\dim(X_{1})+m=\dim(X_{2})
, and
m\geq 0
then the induced embeddings
Y\subseteq X_{1}\subseteq{\mathbf{A}}^{m}_{X_{1}}
and
Y\subset X_{2}
are ’etale equivalent. Here the embedding

X_{1}\subseteq{\mathbf{A}}^{m}_{X_{1}}=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}])

is defined by
V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})
.

For any local embeddings
Y\subset X_{1}=V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\subset{\mathbf{A}}^{m}_{X_{1}}
and
Y\subset X_{2}
with
\dim(X_{1})+m=\dim(X_{2})
we get that
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})
is partial maximal contact in gradation
t
.
Then passing to the completion
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}
the maximal admissibility condition

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}\cdot R_{Y}=\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}[(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})t,R_{Y|X_{1}}]\subset\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}\cdot{\mathcal{A}}:=\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,p}[\overline{x}_{1}t,\ldots\overline{x}_{k}t^{1/a_{k}}],

with
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\subset\overline{x}_{1}
is equivalent to the maximal admissibility
R_{Y|X_{1}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{Y|X_{1}}
, and thus

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R_{Y,{\mathbf{A}}^{m}_{X_{1}}})=(1,\ldots,1,{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}(R_{Y|X_{1}})=

=(1,\ldots,1,{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y,X_{1}}).

Moreover, by functoriality, we have

{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y,{X_{2}}})={\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y,{\mathbf{A}}^{m}_{X_{1}}})=(1,\ldots,1,{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y,X_{1}})),

For a closed embedding
Y\subset X
into a smooth
X
of dimension
n
, and
p\in Y
, let
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y})={\operatorname{inv}}(b_{1},\ldots,b_{k})
. We define the invariant
\widetilde{{\operatorname{inv}}}_{p}(Y)
on
Y
to be the equivalence class of the sequences
(b_{1},\ldots,b_{k})_{n}
, marked by
n=\dim(X)
, where
Y\subset X
and
{\operatorname{inv}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y})=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{k})
, with the equivalence relation

(b_{1},\ldots,b_{k})_{n}\simeq(1,\ldots,1,b_{1},\ldots,b_{k})_{n+m},

where
m
is the
number of ones in front of
b_{1}
. By the above the invariant is independent of embeddings and functorial for smooth morphisms.

One can compare two equivalence classes lexicographically, as before, by fixing their representatives with the same marking
n
.

We shall use the following

Lemma 3.9.4
.

Let
Y\subset X_{1}
, and
Y\subset X_{2}
be two different embeddings. For a point
p\in Y
consider two maximal admissible centers at
p
:
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
for
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y,X_{1}}
on
X_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
for
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y,X_{1}}
.
Denote by
B_{1+}\to X_{1}
, and
B_{2+}\to X_{2}
the cobordant blow-ups respectively at
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
,
Then

{\mathcal{A}}_{1|Y}={\mathcal{A}}_{2|Y},

and consequently the restrictions of the cobordant blow-ups
B_{1+}\to X_{1}
, and
B_{2+}\to X_{2}
to the strict transforms of
Y
coincide with the cobordant blow-up
B_{Y+}\to Y
of
{\mathcal{A}}_{1|Y}={\mathcal{A}}_{2|Y}
on
Y
.

Proof.

We can embed further
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
into an affine space
{\mathbb{A}}^{N}
in such a way that the induced embeddings of
Y
to
{\mathbb{A}}^{N}
coincide (see for intance
[
Wło05
, Lemma 2.5.3]
). This reduces the situation to the embedding
X_{1}\subset X_{2}={\mathbb{A}}^{N}
.

Now
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y,X_{1}}={\mathcal{I}}_{Y,X_{2}|X_{1}}
, and similarly
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}={\mathcal{A}}_{2|X_{1}}
implying
{\mathcal{A}}_{1|Y}={\mathcal{A}}_{2|Y}
on
Y
. Consequently the morphism on the strict transforms
\sigma^{c}(Y)\to Y
on
X_{1}
or
X_{2}
coincides, by Lemma
5.1.5
, with the cobordant blow-up
of
{\mathcal{A}}_{1|Y}={\mathcal{A}}_{2|Y}
.

∎

We shall run the embedded algorithm using the centers associated with
\max\widetilde{{\operatorname{inv}}}
on
Y
. It locally corresponds to the center
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}
associated with
{\operatorname{maxinv}}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y})
for a certain embedding
Y\subset X
.

Consider an open affine cover of
Y
by
Y^{j}
admitting closed embeddings
Y^{j}\subset X^{j}
into smooth varieties
X^{j}
. Let
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y^{j}}
be the corresponding ideal on
X^{j}
. We can assume that all
X^{j}
are of the same dimension. Let

\overline{Y}:=\coprod Y_{j}\hookrightarrow\overline{X}:=\coprod X_{j}

be the induced embedding of disjoint unions. Consider the natural étale projection
\overline{Y}\to Y
.

Now we shall run the embedded desingularization with the strict transforms for
\overline{Y}\hookrightarrow\overline{X}
, that the sequence of maximal
{\mathcal{I}}_{\overline{Y}_{i}}
-admissible centers
\overline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{i}
:

\overline{X}=\overline{X}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{0}}}{{\leftarrow}}\overline{X}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{1}}}{{\leftarrow}}\ldots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{k-1}}}{{\leftarrow}}\overline{X}_{k}=\overline{X}^{\prime},

with ideals
{\mathcal{I}}_{\overline{Y}_{i}}
being the strict transform of
{\mathcal{I}}_{\overline{Y}_{i-1}}
, which
induces the sequence of the strict transforms

\overline{Y}=\overline{Y}_{0\overline{Y}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{1\overline{Y}}}}{{\leftarrow}}\overline{Y}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{{1}\overline{Y}}}}{{\leftarrow}}\ldots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{k-1\overline{Y}}}}{{\leftarrow}}\overline{Y}_{k}=\overline{Y}^{\prime},

The latter is the sequence of the cobordant blow-ups with the
induced centers
\overline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{i|\overline{Y}_{1}}
. By functoriality and Lemma
3.9.4
, it descends to the sequence of the induced cobordant blow-ups

{Y}={Y}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{0Y}}}{{\leftarrow}}{Y}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{1Y}}}{{\leftarrow}}\ldots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{k-1Y}}}{{\leftarrow}}{Y}_{k}={Y}^{\prime},

on
Y
.
Then,
\max\widetilde{{\operatorname{inv}}}
drops to the minimal value
(1,1,\ldots,1)_{n}
on
\overline{Y}^{\prime}
and on
Y^{\prime}
, where
n
is the dimension of the ambient variety
\overline{X}
, and the number of ones is equal to the codimension of the smooth subvariety
\overline{Y}\subset\overline{X}
.

The exceptional SNC divisor on a local ambient space
\overline{X}^{\prime}
is compatible with a smooth subvariety
\overline{Y}^{\prime}
. Consequently, its restriction to
\overline{Y}^{\prime}
defines an SNC-exceptional divisor on
\overline{Y}^{\prime}
and determines a functorial nonembedded SNC resolution of
\overline{Y}
, while its descent determines a functorial nonembedded SNC resolution of
{Y}
. This completes the proof of Theorem
1.11.7
.
A similar non-SNC resolution was considered in
[
ATW19
]
,

4.
Free characteristic resolution of almost homogenous singularities

4.1.
The weighted normal bundles to the centers

4.1.1.
Weighted normal bundle

Let
X
be a regular scheme.

Let
{\mathcal{J}}
be a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-ideal
locally written as
{\mathcal{J}}=(u_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,u_{k}^{1/w_{k}})
, and let
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{J}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1},u_{1}t^{w_{1}},\ldots,u_{k}t^{w_{k}}]^{\operatorname{int}}
be the associated Rees algebra.
Once can consider the filtration

\{({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}\}_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}=\{{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{J}},a}\}_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}

of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
.
Then
{\operatorname{gr}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})
is a sheaf of graded
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}/{\mathcal{J}}_{X}={\mathcal{O}}_{V({\mathcal{J}})}
-modules on
V({\mathcal{J}})
, which is locally on
X
, isomorphic to the graded ring

\displaystyle{\operatorname{gr}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})={\mathcal{O}}_{X}/{\mathcal{J}}_{X}\oplus({\mathcal{J}}_{X}/{\mathcal{J}}^{2}_{X})t\oplus\ldots\oplus({\mathcal{J}}^{i}_{X}/{\mathcal{J}}^{i+1})_{X}t^{i}\oplus\ldots=

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{O}}_{X}\oplus({\mathcal{J}}_{X})t\oplus\ldots\oplus({\mathcal{J}}^{i}_{X}/t^{i}\oplus\ldots)\,\,/\penalty\,\,({\mathcal{J}}_{X}\oplus\ldots\oplus({\mathcal{J}}^{i}_{X})t^{i-1}\oplus\ldots=

\displaystyle={\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}[t^{-1}]/(t^{-1}{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}[t^{-1}])={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{J}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}/(t^{-1}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{J}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}})={\mathcal{O}}_{B}/(t^{-1}\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{B})=

\displaystyle={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1},u_{1}t^{w_{1}},\ldots,u_{k}t^{w_{k}}]/(t^{-1})={\mathcal{O}}_{V({\mathcal{J}})}[u_{1}t^{w_{1}},\ldots,u_{k}t^{w_{k}}],

where
u_{i}
is in
w_{i}
gradation. We shall call the corresponding scheme the
weighted normal bundle
at the center
{\mathcal{J}}
, and denote it by

N_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X):=\operatorname{Spec}({\operatorname{gr}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})).

Remark 4.1.2
.

A similar idea was considered independently in
[
QR22
]
.

4.1.3.
The ideal of the initial forms

With any function
f\in{\mathcal{O}}_{X,p}
, regular at
p\in V({\mathcal{J}})
, such that
f\in(({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}\smallsetminus({\mathcal{J}}^{a+1})_{X})
, for a certain
a\in{\mathbb{N}}
one can associate the unique homogenous element- the initial form

\operatorname{in}(f)\in(({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}/({\mathcal{J}}^{a+1})_{X})t^{a}\subset{\operatorname{gr}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}).

Similarly, we associate with an
ideal sheaf
{\mathcal{I}}
, the filtration
{\mathcal{I}}_{a}:={\mathcal{I}}\cap({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}
, where
a\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and
its ideal of the initial forms on
N_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)
:

\operatorname{in}({\mathcal{I}})=\bigoplus_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}{\geq 0}}({\mathcal{I}}_{a}/({\mathcal{I}}_{a+1})t^{a}=\bigoplus_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}({\mathcal{I}}_{a}+({\mathcal{J}}^{a+1})_{X})/({\mathcal{J}}^{a+1})_{X}t^{a}\subset{\operatorname{gr}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}).

4.1.4.
The weighted normal bundle and the exceptional divisor

The computations above imply the following extension of a classical result of Huneke-Swanson on extended Rees algebras and smooth blow-ups
[
HS06
, Definition 5.1.5]
.

Lemma 4.1.5
.

Let
X
be a regular scheme and
\sigma:B\to X
be the full cobordant blow-up of the center
{\mathcal{J}}
. Then the exceptional divisor
V_{B}(t^{-1})
is isomorphic to
N_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)
:

V_{B}(t^{-1})=\operatorname{Spec}_{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{B}/(t^{-1}))\simeq N_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)

∎

4.1.6.
The strict transform and the ideal of the initial form

We will need the above identification in the context of the strict transforms of the ideals.

Lemma 4.1.7
.

With the previous assumptions and notation. Let
{\mathcal{I}}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
be an ideal sheaf on
X
.
Let
\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}})\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{B}
be the strict transform of
{\mathcal{I}}
.
Then the natural isomorphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{B}/(t^{-1})\to{\operatorname{gr}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})
takes

\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}})_{|V(t^{-1})}
to
\operatorname{in}({\mathcal{I}})
.

Proof.

Let
f\in{\mathcal{I}}
such that
f\in({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}\smallsetminus({\mathcal{J}}^{{a}+1})_{X}
.
Then the strict transform
\sigma^{s}(f)=t^{a}f\in({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}t^{a}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{B}
, and its reduction modulo
t^{-1}
is in
{\mathcal{O}}_{B}/(t^{-1}\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{B})={\operatorname{gr}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})
in the gradation

({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}t^{a}/(t^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{B}\cap{\mathcal{O}}_{X}t^{a})=(({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}/({\mathcal{J}}^{{a}+1})_{X})\cdot t^{a},

and naturally and bijectively corresponds to
\operatorname{in}(f)\in({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}/({\mathcal{J}}^{{a}+1})_{X}t^{a}\subset{\operatorname{gr}}_{\mathcal{J}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}).

∎

4.1.8.
Weighted normal cone

One can extend the construction of the weighted normal bundle of a center
{\mathcal{J}}
on a regular scheme to the weighted normal cone at any Rees algebra
R=\bigoplus_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}R_{a}t^{a}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t]
on any noetherian scheme
X
. Consider the associated gradation

{\operatorname{gr}}_{R}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})=\bigoplus_{a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}((R_{a})_{X}/(R_{a+1})_{X})t^{a}=R/(R\cap t^{-1}R).

Then by
the weighted normal cone of
X
at
R

we shall mean

C_{R}(X):=\operatorname{Spec}_{V(R)}({\operatorname{gr}}_{R}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}))

Definition 4.1.9
.

Let
X
be a regular scheme, and

Y\subset X
be an integral, closed subscheme with an ideal sheaf
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}
. Let
{\mathcal{J}}
be a center on
X
with
V({\mathcal{J}})\subset Y
. Denote by

R_{{\mathcal{J}},Y}:={\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\cdot{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}={\mathcal{O}}_{Y}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[{\mathcal{J}}t]_{X})

the induced Rees algebra on

Y
. Then by the
weighted normal cone
of
Y
at
V({\mathcal{J}})
we mean

C_{{\mathcal{J}}}(Y):=\operatorname{Spec}_{V({\mathcal{J}})}(C_{R_{{\mathcal{J}},Y}}(Y))=\operatorname{Spec}_{V(R)}({\operatorname{gr}}_{R_{{\mathcal{J}},Y}}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})).

Lemma 4.1.10
.

With the above notation and assumptions

C_{{\mathcal{J}}}(Y)=\operatorname{Spec}_{V(R)}({\operatorname{gr}}_{R_{{\mathcal{J}},Y}}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y}))

is the closed subscheme of the weighted normal bundle
{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)
which is defined by
\operatorname{in}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y})\subset{\operatorname{gr}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})
.

Proof.

Consider the surjective morphism of sheaves

\phi:{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}\to R_{{\mathcal{J}},Y}
. Its kernel is generated by
({\mathcal{I}}_{Y}\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t])\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}
. So

R_{{\mathcal{J}},Y}\simeq\bigoplus\frac{({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}}{{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}\cap({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}}\cdot t^{a}.

The morphism
\phi
defines the surjective morphism

{\operatorname{gr}}_{{\mathcal{J}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})=\bigoplus({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}/({\mathcal{J}}^{a+1})_{X}t^{a}\to{\operatorname{gr}}_{R}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})=\bigoplus\frac{({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X}}{({\mathcal{J}}^{a+1})_{X}+({\mathcal{I}}_{Y}\cap({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X})}\cdot t^{a}.

Its kernel is exactly

\operatorname{in}({\mathcal{I}}_{Y})=\bigoplus\frac{(({\mathcal{I}}_{Y}\cap({\mathcal{J}}^{a})_{X})+({\mathcal{J}}^{a+1})_{X}}{({\mathcal{J}}^{a+1})_{X}}\cdot t^{a}.

∎

Remark 4.1.11
.

Assume that
X
is a variety over an algebraically closed field
K
and
B
is the cobordant blow-up of a center
{\mathcal{J}}
. It follows from Lemmas
4.1.5
,
4.1.7
, that when considering the natural projection

\pi:B\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}=\operatorname{Spec}(K[t^{-1}]
we obtain the
deformation of the
X=\pi^{-1}(a)
, where
a\neq 0
to the the normal weighted cone
N_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)=\pi^{-1}(0)=V_{B}(t^{-1})
. Its restriction to
\pi_{C}:C:=V(\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}}))\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
determines the
deformation of
Y
to the normal weighted cone
C_{{\mathcal{J}}}(Y)
, with
Y=\pi_{C}^{-1}(a)
for
a\neq 0
and
C_{{\mathcal{J}}}(Y)=\pi_{C}^{-1}(0)=V_{C}(t^{-1})
. This observation was made by Quek and Rydh in
[
QR22
]
.

4.2.
Almost homogenous singularities and their resolution

For any ideal
{\mathcal{I}}
on a regular scheme
X
let

{\operatorname{Sing}}(V({\mathcal{I}})):={\operatorname{Sing}}(\operatorname{Spec}_{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}/{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}))

denote the singular locus of the scheme
\operatorname{Spec}_{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}/{\mathcal{I}}_{Y})
.

Definition 4.2.1
.

Let
X
be a regular scheme.
Let
Y\subset X
be an integral closed subscheme with an ideal sheaf
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}
. We say that a regular subscheme
Z\subset Y
is an
almost homogenous singularity
of
Y
if

(1)

The singular locus of
Y
is
{\operatorname{Sing}}(Y)=Z
.

(2)

There is a center
{\mathcal{J}}
on
X
, such that
V({\mathcal{J}})=Z
, and

{\operatorname{Sing}}(C_{{\mathcal{J}}}(Y))=V(\operatorname{in}({\mathcal{J}}))=Z\subseteq N_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X).

Theorem 4.2.2
.

Let
X
be a regular scheme.
Let
Y\subset X
be an integral closed subscheme. Let
Z\subset Y
be an almost homogenous singularity of
Y
for a center
{\mathcal{J}}
. Let
N_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)
be the weighted normal bundle of
X
at
{\mathcal{J}}
, and
C_{{\mathcal{J}}}(Y)\subset N_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)
be the weighted normal cone of
Y
at
{\mathcal{J}}
. Assume that either
X
is universally catenary or the codimension of each component of

C_{{\mathcal{J}}}(Y)\smallsetminus Z
in
N_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)
is equal to the codimension of
Y
in
X
.

Then the cobordant blow-up
B_{+}\to X
defines a cobordant resolution of
Y
. That is, the strict transform
Y^{\prime}
of
Y
is a regular subscheme of
B_{+}
of the codimension equal to the codimension of
Y
in
X
.

Proof.

The problem is local on
X
. Thus we can assume that
{\mathcal{J}}=(u_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,u_{k}^{1/w_{k}})
and the full cobordant blow-up of
{\mathcal{J}}
is given by

\sigma:B=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1},t^{w}_{1}u_{1},\ldots,t^{w_{k}}u_{k}]\to X.

Assume that
X
is universally catenary, and thus
B
is catenary.
Let
d
be the codimension of
Y
in
X
.
Then for the morphism
\sigma_{-}:B_{-}=X\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to X
, the inverse image
\sigma_{-}^{-1}(Y)
is irreducible of codimension
d
. So it is its closure
Y^{\prime}:=\overline{\sigma^{-1}(Y)}
