, which is the strict transform of
Y
:

\operatorname{codim}_{B}(Y^{\prime})=d

Note that by the definition of the strict transform,
t^{-1}
is not a zero divisor of
{\mathcal{O}}_{B}/{\mathcal{I}}_{Y^{\prime}}
.
Then, by the Krull Hauptidealsatz, we have that each component of
Y^{\prime}\cap V(t^{-1})
is of codimension
1
in
Y^{\prime}
, and the codimension
d+1
in
B
. We conclude that each component of
Y^{\prime}\cap V(t^{-1})
is of codimension
d
in
V(t^{-1})
.

Note that
{\operatorname{Sing}}(Y^{\prime})\smallsetminus V(t^{-1})={\operatorname{Sing}}(Y^{\prime})\cap B_{-}
is contained in

V_{B_{-}}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k})=V_{B_{-}}(u_{1}t^{w_{1}},\ldots,u_{k}t^{w_{k}})=V_{B_{-}}(u^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,u^{\prime}_{k}),

where
u_{i}^{\prime}=u_{i}t^{w_{i}}
.
On the other hand, by the assumption,

{\operatorname{Sing}}(\operatorname{in}(C_{\mathcal{J}}(Y))\subset V(u_{1}t^{w_{1}},\ldots,u_{n}t^{w_{n}})

so via isomorphism from and Lemma
4.1.7
we obtain

{\operatorname{Sing}}(Y^{\prime}\cap V_{B}(t^{-1}))\subset V_{B}(u_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,u_{n}^{\prime})\cap V_{B}(t^{-1}).

Then, by the assumption on codimension for any point
p\in(Y^{\prime}\cap V(t^{-1}))\smallsetminus V(u_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,u_{n}^{\prime})
, we can find parameters
v_{1},\ldots v_{d}\in({\mathcal{O}}_{B}/t^{-1})\cdot{\mathcal{I}}_{Y^{\prime}}
at
p
which vanish on
Y^{\prime}\cap V(t^{-1})
. But these parameters come from local parameters in
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y^{\prime}}
on
B
at
p
. So they define a regular subscheme
Y^{\prime\prime}
of
B
of codimension
d
, containing locally
Y^{\prime}
. Thus
Y^{\prime\prime}
locally coincides with
Y^{\prime}
which must be regular at
p\in V(t^{-1})\smallsetminus V_{B}(u_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,u_{n}^{\prime})
.
Consequently
{\operatorname{Sing}}(Y^{\prime})
is contained in
V_{B}(u_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,u_{n}^{\prime})
, and
Y^{\prime}
is a regular subscheme of
B_{+}=B\smallsetminus V_{B}(u_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,u_{n}^{\prime})
of codimension
d
.

∎

As a corollary from Theorem
4.2.2
, we obtain the following:

Theorem 4.2.3
.

Let
X
be a smooth scheme over a field
K
of any characteristic.
Let
Y\subset X
be a closed integral subscheme of
X
admitting an almost homogenous singularity
Z\subset Y
.
There is a resolution of
Y
at
Z
, that is, a projective birational morphism
\phi:Y^{res}\to Y
from a smooth variety
Y^{res}
with the exceptional locus
Z\subset Y
, such that
\phi^{-1}(Z)
is an SNC divisor on
Y^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Take the cobordant resolution
B_{+}\to X
from Theorem
4.2.2
. By Corollary
5.2.9
, the geometric quotient
B_{+}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
is locally toric, and thus it can be canonically resolved by the combinatorial method of
[
Wło20
, Theorem 7.17.1]
. This produces the projective birational resolution
Y^{\prime}\to Y
of
Y
such that the inverse image of the singular point is an SNC divisor.

∎

A typical situation where the theorem can be applied is an isolated singularity.

Example 4.2.4
.

In Example

3.2.2
with the singularity

f=x_{1}^{c_{1}}+\ldots+x_{n}^{c_{n}}

we can assume that the characteristic of the field is nonzero and the singular locus is the origin
V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
. Then the the cobordant blow-up of
{\mathcal{J}}=(x_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,x_{n}^{1/w_{n}})
with
w_{1}c_{1}=\ldots=w_{n}c_{n}
resolves the singularity.
The same is still valid if

\operatorname{in}_{{\mathcal{J}}}(f)=x_{1}^{c_{1}}+\ldots+x_{n}^{c_{n}},

and we assume that both
{\operatorname{Sing}}(V(\operatorname{in}_{{\mathcal{J}}}(f))
and
{\operatorname{Sing}}(V(f))
is the origin.

Example 4.2.5
.

Consider the mixed characteristic hypersurface
Y
in
X={\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{2}\smallsetminus V(k)
, where
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, defined by

f=x^{p}+p^{p}+y^{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[1/k][x,y],

with
p\not|k
. Taking the derivative of the ideal
(f)
over
{\mathbb{Z}}[1/k]
we get

{\mathcal{D}}(f)=(x^{p}+p^{p}+y^{k},px^{p-1},ky^{k-1})

we see that
{\operatorname{Sing}}(f)\subseteq V(x^{p}+p^{p}+y^{k},px^{p-1},ky^{k-1})=V(x,y,p)
.
Thus
Y:=V(f)
has an isolated singularity
V(x,y,p)
.
After the coordinate change
x^{\prime}:=x+p
we get the equation:

f={x^{\prime}}^{p}-p\cdot p{x^{\prime}}^{p-1}+\ldots+p\cdot p^{p-1}x^{\prime}+y^{k}

Consider the center

{{\mathcal{J}}}:=((x^{\prime})^{1/w_{1}},p^{1/w_{2}},y^{1/w_{3}})

with the integral weights satisfying

pw_{1}=pw_{2}+w_{1}=kw_{3}.

We obtain the initial
form

\operatorname{in}f={x^{\prime}}^{p}+p^{p}x^{\prime}+y^{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[x^{\prime}t^{w_{1}},pt^{w_{2}},zt^{w_{3}}].

Here
x^{\prime},p,y
correspond to the graded elements
x^{\prime}t^{w_{1}},pt^{w_{2}},zt^{w_{3}}
.
Taking derivatives over
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
with respect to the free variables
x^{\prime},p,y
on
Z_{p}[x^{\prime},p,z]
we obtain

{\mathcal{D}}(\operatorname{in}f)=({x^{\prime}}^{p}+p^{p}x^{\prime}+y^{k},p^{p},ky^{k-1}).

This implies that

{\operatorname{Sing}}(\operatorname{in}(f))\subset V({x^{\prime}}^{p}+p^{p}x^{\prime}+y^{k},p^{p},ky^{k-1})=V(x^{\prime},y,p)=V({{\mathcal{J}}}).

Thus
f
defines an almost homogenous singularity for
{\mathcal{J}}
and can be resolved by a single cobordant blow-up of
{{\mathcal{J}}}
.

Observe that the seemingly natural center
{{\mathcal{J}}}_{1}=(x^{1/k},p^{1/k},y^{1/p})
does not define an almost homogenous singularity (for
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
), and the corresponding cobordant blow-up does not resolve singularity. In such a case

\operatorname{in}(f)=x^{p}+p^{p}+y^{k}=(x+p)^{p}+z^{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[x,y,p],

whence
{\operatorname{Sing}}(\operatorname{in}(f))=V(x+p,z)\supsetneq V({\mathcal{J}}_{1})
. This also motivates the coordinate change above.

4.3.
Partial resolution by the invariants

The method can be linked to different invariants and is related to the characteristic zero method.

For any ideal
{\mathcal{I}}
on a regular scheme
X
, and an integer
d
let

{\operatorname{supp}}({\mathcal{I}},d):=\{p\in X\mid{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}})\leq d\}.

Theorem 4.3.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{I}}
be an ideal on a regular scheme
X
. Let
d
be any natural integer. Assume that there exists a center
{{\mathcal{J}}}
such that

(1)

{\operatorname{supp}}({\mathcal{I}},d)=V({\mathcal{J}})\subset X,\quad\mbox{and}

(2)

{\operatorname{supp}}(\operatorname{in}({\mathcal{I}}),d)=V({\mathcal{J}})\subset C_{J}(X)
.

Then for the cobordant blow-up
\sigma_{+}:B_{+}\to X
of
{\mathcal{J}}
, we have:

{\operatorname{ord}}_{B_{+}}(\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}}))<d

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{J}}=(x_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}^{1/w_{k}})
be the center.
If
q\in V(t^{-1})\smallsetminus V(x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k})
, then

{\operatorname{ord}}_{q}(\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}})\leq{\operatorname{ord}}_{q}(\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}})_{|V(t^{-1})})={\operatorname{ord}}_{q}(\operatorname{in}({\mathcal{I}}))<d.

If

q\in B\smallsetminus V(t^{-1})\smallsetminus V(x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k})=B_{-}\smallsetminus V(x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k})=(X\smallsetminus V({\mathcal{J}}))\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, then since
\sigma(q)\in X\smallsetminus V({\mathcal{J}})
we conclude that

{\operatorname{ord}}_{q}(\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}}))={\operatorname{ord}}_{\sigma(q)}({\mathcal{I}})<d.

∎

4.4.
Resolution principle in any characteristic

In practice, one can replace
"{\operatorname{ord}}"
with a different type of invariants satisfying the properties used in the proof of the above lemma,
like some modifications of the Hilbert-Samuel function or
{\operatorname{inv}}
and
{\operatorname{inv}}_{E}
for smooth schemes in characteristic zero or certain schemes in positive or mixed characteristic. One can link it to a more general principle:

Let
\sigma:B\to X
be a full cobordant blow-up of a center
{\mathcal{J}}
. Let
Y\subset X
be a closed subscheme. Let
D=V_{B}(t^{-1})
be the exceptional divisor of
\pi
.

•

The behavior of
the strict transform
\sigma^{s}(Y)
at the part of the vertex
{\operatorname{Vert}}(B)\smallsetminus D
of
B
is controlled by the behavior of
Y
at
V({\mathcal{J}})
.

•

The behavior of the strict transform
\sigma^{s}(Y)
at the part of the vertex
{\operatorname{Vert}}(B)\cap D
is to a great extent controlled by the
weighted normal cone
C_{{\mathcal{J}}}(Y)
to the center
V({\mathcal{J}})
.

Example 4.4.1
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
p>2
.
Let
Y\subset X=\operatorname{Spec}k[x,y,z]
be a closed subscheme
defined by

f=x^{p}+y^{p}z+z^{k}+x^{p-1}y^{2}+x^{p+1}yz,

of order
p>2
with
p\not|k(k-1)
,
k\geq 2p+1
.
Computing
{\mathcal{D}}^{2}(f)
we obtain that

{\operatorname{supp}}(f,3)=V({\mathcal{D}}^{2}(f))=V(x,y,z).

Then by solving the equations for the weights

pw_{1}=pw_{2}+w_{3}=kw_{3}

we obtain that the initial form with respect to
{\mathcal{J}}=(x^{1/w_{1}},y^{1/w_{3}},z^{1/w_{3}})
is given by

\operatorname{in}(f)=x^{p}+y^{p}z+z^{k}.

Then
by the similar argument as before

{\operatorname{supp}}(\operatorname{in}(f),3)=V(x,y,z)=V({\mathcal{J}})\subset C_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)

The cobordant blow-up at
{\mathcal{J}}
decreases the maximal order to
2
, and the equation can be solved by further cobordant blow-ups.

Observe that if one considers ”characteristic zero” center

{\mathcal{J}}_{1}:=(x^{\frac{1}{p+1}},y^{\frac{1}{p}},z^{\frac{1}{p}})
associated with maximal admissible Rees center
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[xt^{1/p},yt^{1/(p+1)},z^{1/(p+1)})]
then

\operatorname{in}(f)=x^{p}+y^{p}z
, and

{\operatorname{supp}}(\operatorname{in}(f),3)=V(x,y)\neq V({\mathcal{J}}_{1})

In fact the full cobordant blow-up
B^{\prime}_{+}\to X
of
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
transforms
f
into

x^{p}+y^{p}z+t^{(p+1)-k}z^{k}+t^{1-p}x^{p-1}y^{2}+t^{-3p-1}x^{p+1}yz

On
B_{+}=B\smallsetminus V(x,y,z)
in the chart
B\smallsetminus V(z)
,
the maximal order does not decrease:

{\operatorname{maxord}}_{B_{+}}(\sigma^{s}(f))=p.

4.5.
Homogenous subschemes

Let
V
be a regular scheme and

X:={\mathbb{A}}^{n}_{V}=\operatorname{Spec}_{V}({\mathcal{O}}_{V}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]))

be an affine space over
V
. Let
{\mathcal{J}}=(x_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}^{1/w_{k}})
be a center on
X
.
The center
{\mathcal{J}}
defines the gradation on
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
via the isomorphism
x_{i}\mapsto t^{w_{i}}x_{i}
for
i\leq k
,
x_{i}\mapsto x_{i}
:

\phi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}[t^{w_{1}}x_{1},\ldots,t^{w_{k}}x_{k},x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{n}]

It takes the monomial
x^{\alpha}=x_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdot\ldots\cdot x_{n}^{a_{n}}\in{\mathcal{J}}^{a_{1}w_{1}+\ldots+a_{k}w_{k}}_{X}
to
t^{a_{1}w_{1}+\ldots+a_{k}w_{k}}\cdot x^{\alpha}
.

Let
f\in{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{X}\smallsetminus{\mathcal{J}}^{a+1}_{X}
be a homogenous element in
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}={\mathcal{O}}_{V}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
of grading
a
. Then

\phi(f)=t^{a}f\in{\mathcal{O}}_{V}[t^{w_{1}}x_{1},\ldots,t^{w_{k}}x_{k},x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{n}].

The latter can be interpreted as the strict transform of
f
inside of the cobordant algebra
{\mathcal{O}}_{B}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1},t^{w_{1}}x_{1},\ldots,t^{w_{k}}x_{k}]
.

Definition 4.5.1
.

With the above notation and assumption any subscheme of
X
defined by a homogenous ideal
{\mathcal{I}}
will be called a
homogenous subscheme
with respect to the center
{\mathcal{J}}
.

This leads to a simple but useful result.

Lemma 4.5.2
.

With the assumption and notation above.
Let
{\mathcal{I}}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{V}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
be a homogenous ideal with respect to
{\mathcal{J}}
. Let
\sigma:B\to X
be the cobordant blow-up of
{\mathcal{J}}
, and
\pi:Y:=X\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\to X
be the standard projection. Then

B=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1},t^{w_{1}}x_{1},\ldots,t^{w_{k}}x_{k}]=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}}_{V}[t^{-1},t^{w_{1}}x_{1},\ldots,t^{w_{k}}x_{k},x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{n}]

Thus there exists an isomorphism

B\simeq Y=X\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

over
V
, defined by

B=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}}_{V}[t^{-1},t^{w_{1}}x_{1},\ldots,t^{w_{k}}x_{k},x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{n}]\to Y=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}}_{V}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y])

which takes

•

x_{i}\mapsto t^{w_{i}}x_{i}
for
i\leq k
,

•

x_{i}\mapsto x_{i}
,
i>k

•

y\mapsto t^{-1}
,

•

{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\cdot{\mathcal{I}}
to the strict transform ideal
\sigma_{B}^{s}({\mathcal{I}})

•

V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
to the vertex
V
of
B

∎

Example 4.5.3
.

The lemma explains the transformation of the singularity

f=x_{1}^{c_{1}}+\ldots+x_{k}^{c_{k}},

from Example
3.2.2
which is taken to

\sigma^{c}(f)=\sigma^{s}(f)=(x^{\prime}_{1})^{c_{1}}+\ldots+(x^{\prime}_{k})^{c_{k}}.

Example 4.5.4
.

The following example is due to Narasimhman
[
Nar83
]
.
Let

f=x^{2}+yz^{3}+zw^{3}+y^{7}w\in k[x,y,z,w],

where
k
is a field of characteristic
2
. Its singular locus
{\operatorname{Sing}}(f)
coincides with

{\operatorname{supp}}(f,2)=V(D(f))=V(f,Y,Z,W),

where

Y:=D_{y}(f)=z^{3}+y^{6}w,\quad Z:=D_{z}(f)=yz^{2}+w^{3},\quad W:=D_{w}(f)=zw^{2}+y^{7}.

Note that
F:=f+yY+zZ=x^{2}+yz^{3}
is again binomial. Hence
{\operatorname{Sing}}(f)=V(F,Y,Z,W)
is binomial, so it is a (non-normal) toric subvariety of
{\mathbb{A}}^{4}
of dimension
1
. One can compute that it coincides with the image
t\mapsto(t^{32},t^{7},t^{19},t^{15})
.

Observe that the subscheme
V(f)
is homogenous with respect to the center

{\mathcal{J}}:=(x^{1/32},y^{1/7},z^{1/19},t^{1/15}).

Thus, by Lemma
4.5.2
, the cobordant blow-up of
{\mathcal{J}}
transforms
X
into
B\simeq X\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{k}
with the same equation
f
describing
\sigma^{s}(f)
. Then
B_{+}=B\smallsetminus V(x,y,z,w)
.
The singular locus of
\sigma^{s}(f)
on
B_{+}
is simply the product

\{(t^{32},t^{7},t^{19},t^{15})\mid t\neq 0\}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}.

The functions
Y
,
Z
, and
W
are semiinvariant on
B
. Moreover
D_{z}(Y)=z^{2}
,
D_{w}(Z)=w^{2}
, and
D_{y}(W)=y^{6}
are invertible on an open subset
B_{yzw}=B\smallsetminus V(yzw)
. On the other hand
D_{y}(Y)=D_{z}(Z)=D_{w}(W)\equiv 0
.
Thus any two of
Y,Z,W
form a partial system of local parameters on
B_{yzw}
. Furthermore the set
B_{yzw}
contains

{\operatorname{Sing}}(\sigma^{s}(f))\cap B_{+}=V(\sigma^{s}(f),Y,Z,W)\cap B_{+}=V(\sigma^{s}(f),Y,Z,W)\smallsetminus V(x,y,z,w),

as
yzw
is invertible on
\{(t^{32},t^{7},t^{19},t^{15})\mid t\neq 0\}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.
In addition we can write on
B_{yzw}
:

\sigma^{s}(f)=x^{2}+\frac{1}{z^{2}}YZ+\frac{y^{6}w^{4}}{z^{2}}=X^{2}+\frac{1}{z^{2}}YZ,

where
X:=x+\frac{y^{3}w^{2}}{z}
,
z
is a unit, and
X,Y,Z
form a partial system of local parameters.
The subscheme
V_{B_{+}}(\sigma^{s}(f))
is almost homogenous on
B_{xyz}\subset B_{+}
with respect to the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-stable smooth center described as

{\mathcal{J}}_{1}:=(X,Y,Z)
on
B_{xyz}\subset B_{+}
. In fact,
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
is an ordinary ideal defining a smooth locus
{\operatorname{Sing}}(\sigma^{s}(f))
.
The cobordant (or the ordinary) blow-up of
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
determines a cobordant resolution. Further applying locally toric resolution of
[
Wło20
, Theorem 7.17.1]
resolves the singularity functorially with SNC exceptional divisor.

Alternatively, the singularity can be resolved using the characteristic zero invariant ”
{\operatorname{inv}}
,” but the process is longer and requires three cobordant blow-ups.

Remark 4.5.5
.

The Narasinhman example shows that the maximal contact, or even any regular hypersurface containing equimultiple locus, that is the subset of the points with the equal multiplicity of a function
does not exist. In characteristic zero such property follows immediately from the definition of maximal contact
u\in{\mathcal{D}}^{a-1}({\mathcal{I}})
at a point
p
, where
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}({\mathcal{I}})=a
. In our setting it is a consequence of Lemma
3.6.24
(1).
The equimultiple locus
{\operatorname{supp}}(f,2)={\operatorname{Sing}}(f)
has embedding dimension
4
and cannot be embedded into a smooth hypersurface.
In fact one can show that the invariant ”
{\operatorname{inv}}
” in positive characteristic is not upper semicontinuous, and its locus does not have good properties.

4.5.6.
Cobordant blow-ups vs blow-ups at smooth centers

In the cobordant resolution of the Narasinhman example, the second cobordant blow-up was at the smooth center
(X,Y,Z)
with weights equal to
1
.
In general, we have:

Lemma 4.5.7
.

Let
X
be a regular scheme.
Let
B_{+}\to X
be a cobordant blow-up of
{\mathcal{J}}
, locally given by
{\mathcal{J}}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k})
with weights
1
. Let
Bl_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)\to X
be the ordinary blow-ups of the smooth center
{\mathcal{J}}
. Then the induced quotient morphsim

B_{+}\to B_{+}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\simeq Bl_{{\mathcal{J}}}(X)

is a locally trivial
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-bundle.

Proof.

The problem is local on
X
. Then
B=\operatorname{Spec}_{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1},u_{1}t,\ldots,u_{k}t])
, and
B_{+}
is covered by
B_{u_{i}t^{w}}=B\smallsetminus V(u_{i}t)=\operatorname{Spec}_{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-1},u_{1}t,\ldots,u_{k}t,(u_{i}t)^{-1}])
, and

B_{u_{i}t}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}=\operatorname{Spec}_{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\frac{u_{1}t}{u_{i}t},\ldots,\frac{u_{k}t}{u_{i}t}])=\operatorname{Spec}_{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\frac{u_{1}}{u_{i}},\ldots,\frac{u_{k}}{u_{i}}]).

Thus the induced morphism
B_{+}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to X
is a weighted blow-up which coincides with the standard blow-up
of
{\mathcal{J}}
. Since we have that
t^{-1}=(u_{i}t)^{-1}u_{i}
, we can write

{\mathcal{O}}_{B_{u_{i}t}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\frac{u_{1}}{u_{i}},\ldots,\frac{u_{k}}{u_{i}}])[t^{-1},u_{i}t,(u_{i}t)^{-1}]={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[\frac{u_{1}}{u_{i}},\ldots,\frac{u_{k}}{u_{i}}])[u_{i}t,(u_{i}t)^{-1}].

Thus locally
B_{+}\simeq(Bl_{{\mathcal{J}}})\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.
∎

5.
Appendix

5.1.
Cobordant blow-ups of Rees algebras and generalized weighted centers

5.1.1.
Generalized cobordant blow-ups

Definition 5.1.2
.

Let
R=\bigoplus_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}R_{a}t^{a}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t]
be any Rees algebra on a Noetherian scheme
Y
, and let
R[t^{-1}]
be an extended algebra of
R
. Then

the full cobordant blow-up of
R
is given by
B:=Spec_{X}(R[t^{-1}])\to X
. The
cobordant blow-up
is simply
B_{+}:=B\smallsetminus V(R)
.

5.1.3.
Generalized centers

We generalize the centers accordingly.

Definition 5.1.4
.

A Rees center on a Noetherian scheme
X
is an extended Rees algebra
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}
it can be locally presented as
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-w},t^{1/a_{1}}f_{1},\ldots,t^{1/a_{k}}f_{k}]
, where
V(f_{1},\ldots,f_{k})
is a regular subscheme of
X
.

The following is a simple consequence of the definition:

Lemma 5.1.5
.

If
f:X\to Y
is a morphism of Noetherian schemes, and

R_{Y}
is a Rees algebra on
Y
then
R_{X}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}\cdot R_{Y}
is the induced Rees algebra on
X
. Let
B_{Y}\to Y
and
B_{X}\to X
denote the full cobordant blow-ups of
Y
at
R_{Y}
and
X
at
R_{X}
respectively. Then there are induced morphisms
f_{B}:B_{X}\to B_{Y}
,
f_{B_{+}}:B_{X+}\to B_{Y+}
commuting with cobordant blow-ups to
X
and
Y
. Furtheremore, if
X\to Y
is a closed immersion, then
B_{X}\to B_{Y}
and
f_{B_{+}}:B_{X+}\to B_{Y+}
are so.

Proof.

The morphism
f:X\to Y
determines the homomorphism of graded sheaves
R_{Y}\to f_{*}R_{X}
, so that for any open subset
U\subset X
and
V\subset Y
such that
f(U)\subseteq V
, we have the induced graded homomorphisms
R_{Y}(V)\to R_{X}(U)
which gives rise to
B_{X}\to B_{Y}
taking the vertex
V_{X}
to the vertex
V_{Y}
. If
f
is a closed immersion then
X
can be thought as a closed subscheme of
Y
and we get the induced surjective homomorphism of graded
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
-modules
R_{Y}\to R_{X}
which defines the closed immersions
f_{B}:B_{X}\to B_{Y}
,
f_{B_{+}}:B_{X+}\to B_{Y+}
.
∎

5.1.6.
The maximal admissible centers on the subscheme are regular

Lemma 5.1.7
.

Let
X
be a smooth variety over a field
k
of characteristic zero, and
E
be an SNC divisor on
X
.
Let
Y\subset X
be a reduced closed subscheme of a
X
which is not locally contained in the divisor
E
, and let
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}
be its ideal on
X
. Let
{\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}
be a maximal
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}
-admissible center locally at a point
p\in Y
of the form
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}[t^{-w},t^{1/a_{1}}u_{1}t^{1/a_{k}},\ldots,t^{1/a_{k}}u_{k}]
. Then

{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\cdot{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}}={\mathcal{O}}_{Y}[t^{-w},t^{1/a_{1}}f_{1}t^{1/a_{k}},\ldots,t^{1/a_{k}}f_{k}],

for
f_{i}:=u_{i|Y}
,
is a Rees center on
Y
.

Proof.

By definition
{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}t\subset{\mathcal{A}}^{{\operatorname{ext}}}
. So

Y=V({\mathcal{I}}_{Y})=V({\mathcal{O}}_{X}[{\mathcal{I}}_{Y}t])\supseteq V({\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{ext}})=V(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k})

Consequently

V(f_{1},\ldots,f_{k})=V(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k})_{|Y}=V(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k})

is regular on
Y
.

∎

5.2.
Geometric quotient and cobordant blow-ups

In this section, we review some standard properties of the torus actions, usually stated over algebraically closed fields, and extended here to noetherian schemes.

Definition 5.2.1
.

Let a torus
T={\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{l}
, where
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[t,t^{-1}])
act on a scheme
X
. We say that an action of a torus
T
on scheme
X
is
relatively affine
if for any point
p\in X
admits an open
T
-stable affine neighborhood
U
of
p
.

Let
\pi:X\to Y
be an invariant morphism of schemes. We say that an action of a torus
T
on scheme
X
is
relatively affine over
Y
if for any point
p\in X
there is an open
T
-stable affine neighborhood
U
of
p
, mapping to an open affine neighborhood of
V
of
q=\pi(q)\in Y
, and a
T
-equivariant closed immersion

\phi_{U}:U\hookrightarrow V\times{\mathbf{A}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r},

with the diagonal action of
T
on
{\mathbf{A}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r}
, and commuting with the invariant morphisms

If additionally,
X
admits geometric quotient, and
U
, and
V
can be chosen so that
V\times{\mathbf{A}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r}

admits geometric quotient then we say that
T
admits
relatively affine geometric quotient
over
Y
.

We shall need a well known fact for the action of the torus on any affine schemes.

Lemma 5.2.2
.

Let
X
be an scheme with a relatively affine action of torus
T
, with the group of characters
\chi(T)
. Then
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
admits a natural grading
and

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\chi(T)}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})_{\alpha},

where
T
acts on
{\mathcal{O}}(X)_{\alpha}
by the character
t^{\alpha}
.

Proof.

We can reduce the situation to the case of affine
X
.
The natural grading on
{\mathcal{O}}(X)
, and thus on the sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}

is given by the coaction

\rho:{\mathcal{O}}(X)\to{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}[T],

where
{\mathbb{Z}}[T]={\mathbb{Z}}[t^{\pm}_{1},\ldots,t^{\pm}_{m}]
.
For any
f\in{\mathcal{O}}(X)
,

\rho(f)=\sum c_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}
, where
c_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{O}}(X)
. Moreover it follows from the associativity

(\text{id}_{{\mathcal{O}}(X)}\otimes\mu)\circ\rho=(\rho\times\text{id}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[T]})\circ\rho

where

\mu:{\mathbb{Z}}[T]\to{\mathbb{Z}}[T]\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}[T],\quad\mu(t)=tt^{\prime}

is defined by the group multiplication, such that

\sum c_{\alpha}(tt^{\prime})^{\alpha}=\sum\rho(c_{\alpha})(t^{\prime})^{\alpha},

and hence
\rho(c_{\alpha})=c_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}
. From existence of the identity map
1_{T}:{\mathbb{Z}}[t^{\pm}_{1},\ldots,t^{\pm}_{m}]\to{\mathbb{Z}},
such that

(1_{T}\otimes\text{id}_{{\mathcal{O}}(X)})\circ\rho=\text{id}_{{\mathcal{O}}(X)}

we deduce that

f=(1_{T}\otimes\text{id}_{{\mathcal{O}}(X)})\circ\rho(f)=(1_{T}\otimes\text{id}_{{\mathcal{O}}(X)})(\sum c_{\alpha}t^{\alpha})=\sum c_{\alpha}.

Thus any element decomposes into a finite sum of the homogenous elements
c_{\alpha}
of grading
\alpha
, and

{\mathcal{O}}(X)=\bigoplus{\mathcal{O}}(X)_{\alpha},

where
T
acts on
{\mathcal{O}}(X)_{\alpha}
by the character
t^{\alpha}
. ∎

Definition 5.2.3
.

Let
X
be a scheme with with a relatively affine action of torus
T
. By a
T
-stable ideal
we mean
a homogenous ideal
{\mathcal{I}}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}=\bigoplus_{\alpha}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})_{\alpha}
.

Definition 5.2.4
.

Let
X
be a normal scheme with a relatively affine action of torus
T
.
A
Q
-ideal
{\mathcal{J}}
on a normal scheme is said to be
T
-stable
if is of the form

{\mathcal{J}}={\mathcal{I}}^{1/n}
, where
{\mathcal{I}}
is a
T
-stable ideal.

Lemma 5.2.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{J}}
be a
T
-stable center on a regular scheme
X
with a relatively affine action of
T
, and a
T
-stable SNC divisor
E
. Then one can write locally the center
{\mathcal{J}}
as
{\mathcal{J}}=(x_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}^{1/w_{k}})
, where
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}
is a partial system of semiinvariant local parameters.

Proof.

We can assume that
{\mathcal{J}}
has the form
{\mathcal{J}}=(x_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}^{1/w_{k}})
at a point
p\in X
.
Write
x_{1}\in({\mathcal{J}}^{w_{1}})_{X}
as the sum of homogenous components
x_{1}=\sum c_{\alpha}
. We conclude that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}(c_{\alpha})=1
for some
x^{\prime}_{1}:=c_{\alpha}\in({\mathcal{J}}^{w_{1}})_{X}
. So, by Lemma
3.1.29
, one can replace
x_{1}
with a semiinvariant
x^{\prime}_{1}
in the presentation of
{\mathcal{J}}
. By the induction on dimension, we assume that
{\mathcal{J}}_{|V(x^{\prime}_{1})}=((x^{\prime}_{2})^{1/w_{2}},\ldots,(x^{\prime}_{k})^{1/w_{k}})
is represented by semiinvariant local parameters on
V(x_{1}^{\prime})
. Moreover, there is a surjection of graded rings
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V(x^{\prime}_{1})}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}/V(x^{\prime}_{1})
. Then
x^{\prime}_{i}
can be lifted to the homogenous elements in
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
. Set

{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}:=((x^{\prime}_{1})^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,(x^{\prime}_{k})^{1/w_{k}})=(x^{\prime}_{1})^{1/w_{1}}+((x^{\prime}_{2})^{1/w_{2}},\ldots,(x^{\prime}_{k})^{1/w_{k}}).

Note that such a presentation is independent of the liftings of the elements
x^{\prime}_{i}
,
i\geq 2
on
V(x^{\prime}_{1})
to
U
.
Then
{\mathcal{J}}_{|V(x^{\prime}_{1})}={\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}_{|V(x^{\prime}_{1})}
, and their saturated powers
(({\mathcal{J}}_{|V(x^{\prime}_{1})})^{a}))^{\rm sat}=(({\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}_{|V(x^{\prime}_{1})})^{a})^{\rm sat}
for sufficiently divisible
a
are equal as ideals. Then in the completion

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}_{X,p}}=x^{\prime}_{1}\cdot\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}_{X,p}}\oplus\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}_{X,p}}/(x^{\prime}_{1})

both ideals are equal so

\widehat{{\mathcal{J}}^{a}}:=(x^{\prime}_{1})^{a/w_{1}}+(({\mathcal{J}}_{|V(x^{\prime}_{1})})^{a}))^{\rm sat}=(x^{\prime}_{1})^{a/w_{1}}+(({\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}_{|V(x^{\prime}_{1})})^{a}))^{\rm sat}=\widehat{{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime a}}.

This implies that
{\mathcal{J}}^{a}={\mathcal{J}}^{\prime a}
as ideals and
{\mathcal{J}}={\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}
as
{\mathbb{Q}}
-ideals.
∎

Lemma 5.2.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{I}}_{0}
be an ideal on a regular scheme
X_{0}
, and
X\to X_{0}
be a scheme obtained from
X_{0}
by a sequence of

l
cobordant blow-ups of regular (possibly reducible) weighted centers
T
-stable for the induced torus actions, and
{\mathcal{I}}=\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}})
be its controlled transform.
Then
X
is a regular scheme with the relatively affine action of torus
T={\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{l}
over
X_{0}
and
X
admits a relatively affine geometric quotient
X\to X/T
of
T
over
X_{0}
. Moreover
\sigma^{s}({\mathcal{I}})
is a
T
-stable ideal on
X
.

Proof.

We use the induction on
l
. Let
X\to X_{0}
be a sequence of
l-1
equivariant cobordant blow-ups, and let
B_{+}\to X
be the cobordant blow-up of a
T
-stable center
{{\mathcal{J}}}
.
Let
\pi:X\to X/T^{l-1}
be a
T
-invariant morphism defined by the geometric quotient. Then
B
and
B_{+}
admit an action of
T^{l}:=T^{l-1}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, and

\pi\sigma_{+}:B_{+}\to X/T
is
T^{l}
-invariant.
In order to show that there exists a geometric quotient
B_{+}/T^{l}
it suffices to show that it exists for
\sigma_{+}^{-1}\pi^{-1}(V_{\alpha})
for open affine cover
V_{\alpha}
of
X/T
. By the induction we can assume that each open affine
U:=\pi^{-1}(V_{\alpha})
admits a
T^{l-1}
-equivariant embedding.

\phi:U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\hookrightarrow}}W:=U_{0}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r}

Furthermore the closed image
\phi(U)
is generated by
the seminvariant parameters
v_{1},\ldots,v_{r}\in\Gamma(W,{\mathcal{O}}_{W})
.

Also we can assume that the center on
U
is described as
{\mathcal{J}}=(x_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}^{1/w_{k}})
.
Consider the local presentation of
B
as in Section
2.5.2
.
Let us introduce the free variables denoted by
t^{-1},x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{n}
with the action

\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}}_{U}[t^{-1},x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k}])\smallsetminus V(x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k})=U\times({\mathbb{A}}^{k+1}\smallsetminus V(x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k})),

with the action
of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
on
{\mathbb{A}}^{k+1}=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[t^{-1},x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k}]))
, given by

(t^{-1}\cdot t^{-1},t^{w_{1}}x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,t^{w_{k}}x^{\prime}_{1}),

Then
B_{U}:=\sigma^{-1}(U)
is a closed subscheme of
U\times({\mathbb{A}}^{k+1}\smallsetminus V(x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k}))
:

(B_{U})_{+}=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}}_{U}[t^{-1},x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k}])\smallsetminus V(x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k}))/(t^{-w_{i}}x^{\prime}_{i}-x_{i}).

We consider the action of
T^{l-1}
on
(t^{-1},x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k})
as on the corresponding elements of
B
, so the action of
T^{l-1}
on
t^{-1}
is trivial and the action of
T^{l-1}
on
x^{\prime}_{i}
is the same as on
t^{w_{i}}x_{i}
.

Then for
(B_{U})_{x_{i}}=D(x_{i})=W\smallsetminus V(x_{i})\subset(B_{U})_{+}
we obtain the
T^{l}
-equivariant closed embedding:

(B_{U})_{i}\subset\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}}_{U}[t^{-1},x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{k},(x^{\prime}_{i})^{-1}])=U\times{\mathbb{A}}^{k}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\hookrightarrow U_{0}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{n+k}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r+1},

such that
U_{0}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{n+k}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r+1}
admits geometric quotient of the group action of
T^{l}
.

∎

Proposition 5.2.7
.

Let
Y
be a regular affine scheme. Let
T
act on
{\mathbf{A}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{l}
diagonally so that the geometric quotient exists.
Let
Z
be a closed
T
-stable regular subscheme of
W:=Y\times{\mathbf{A}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{l}
defined by a partial system of semiinvariant local parameters.

Then for any point
p\in Z
there is
an open affine
T
-stable neigbourhood
U\subset Z
of
p
, over an open affine
V\subset Y
, and a
T
-equivariant closed immersion

U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\hookrightarrow}}W_{U}:=W=V\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}\times({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})

for some
a,b,s
, and the
induced fiber square diagram :

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}U_{Z}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\hookrightarrow}}&W=V\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}\times({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}&{\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}\\
\pi_{X}\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\pi_{A}\downarrow\\
U_{Z}/T&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi/T}}{{\hookrightarrow}}&W/Z=(V\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a})\times X_{\sigma}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi/T}}{{\to}}&X_{\sigma}=({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})/T\end{array}.

(1)

T
acts trivially on the factor
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}
.

(2)

T
acts diagonally on the factor
{\mathbf{A}}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}
,
admits a geometric quotient of a diagonal action of
T
, and the action has finite stabilizers.

(3)

\phi
, and
\phi/T
are closed immersions, with their closed images
\phi(U)
, and
\phi_{U/T}(U/T)
is defined by the system of invariant parameters
v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\in(\Gamma(W,{\mathcal{O}}_{W})^{T})
, whose restrictions to

(\pi/T)^{-1}(q_{0})=(V\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a})\times\operatorname{Spec}(k(q_{0}))

also define regular parameters.

Proof.

Let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, for
s\leq n
be the coordinates on
{\mathbb{A}}^{s}
which are noinvertible at
p
on

W_{0}=Y\times{\mathbb{A}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{l}=Y\times{\mathbb{A}}^{s}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{n-s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{l},

and
y_{s+1},\ldots,y_{n+l-s}
are the coordinates on
{\mathbb{A}}^{n-s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{l}
which are invertible at
p
.

We can replace
W
with the open subset
W_{0}=Y\times{\mathbb{A}}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{c}
, where
c:=n-s+l
, and
Z
with
U:=Z\cap W_{0}

First, we show:

Lemma 5.2.8
.

There is an open
T
-stable neighborhood
U
of any point
p
and a fiber square:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}U&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\hookrightarrow}}&W_{U}:=(V\times{\mathbf{A}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{d})\\
\pi_{U}\downarrow&&\pi_{W}\downarrow\\
U/T=\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}}(U)^{T})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi/T}}{{\hookrightarrow}}&W_{U}/T:=V\times({\mathbf{A}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{s})/T\end{array},

for some
d,r\in{\mathbb{N}}
, where
\phi
, and
\phi/T
are closed immersions, with their closed images
\phi(U)
, and
\phi_{U/T}(U/T)
is defined by the system of invariant parameters
v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\in(\Gamma(W,{\mathcal{O}}_{W})^{T})
, whose restrictions to
V\times\{0\}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{d}
also define regular parameters.

Proof.

By the assumptions the image
U\subset W_{0}
, is described locally by a partial system of semiinvariant parameters
v_{1},\ldots,v_{t}
.

Write any semiinvariant parameter
v_{j}
as

v_{j}=\sum c^{j}_{\beta}y^{\beta},+\sum_{\alpha\neq 0}c^{j}_{\alpha\beta}x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}=v_{0}^{j}+w_{0}^{j}

where
c^{j}_{\alpha\beta}\in{\mathcal{O}}(Y)
,
v_{0}^{j}:=\sum c^{j}_{\beta}y^{\beta}
, and
w_{0}^{j}:=\sum_{\alpha\neq 0}c^{j}_{\alpha\beta}x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}
, with all monomials semiinvariant of the same weight.
We will modify the sets of local parameters using the induction on
j
.

Assume that
v_{1},\ldots,v_{j-1}
is the set of first
j-1
local parameters, which are invariant and such that their images in
\frac{m_{p}}{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})+m_{p}^{2}}
are linearly independent.
Consider two cases.

Case 1.
v_{0}^{j}\not\in(v_{1},\ldots,v_{j-1})+(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})+m_{p}^{2}
.

Then multiplying
v_{j}
by the inverse
y^{-\beta}
for some
\beta
occuring in
v_{0}^{j}
we reduce the situation to the invariant parameter
v_{j}^{\prime}:=y^{-\beta}v_{j}
on
U_{0}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{d}
,
such that
v_{1},\ldots,v_{j}
are invariant with their images in
\frac{m_{p}}{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})+m_{p}^{2}}
are linearly independent.

Case 2.
v_{0}^{j}\in(v_{1},\ldots,v_{j-1})+(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})+m_{p}^{2}
.

Then at least one
c^{j}_{\alpha\beta}x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
is a parameter at
p
. Thus
x^{\alpha}=x_{i}
and
u^{j}_{\alpha\beta}:=c^{j}_{\alpha\beta}y^{\beta}
is invertible at
p
, and
c^{j}_{\alpha\beta}
is such.

So, after replacing
U
with
U_{c^{j}_{\alpha\beta}}
, and clearing the unit
v^{\prime}_{j}=(u^{j}_{\alpha\beta})^{-1}v_{j}
we can assume that the semiinvariant parameter
v^{\prime}_{j}
is of the form

v^{\prime}_{j}=x_{i}+h_{j}
, where
h_{i}:=\sum a_{\alpha\beta}x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}
. We can write

v^{\prime}_{j}=x_{i}+h_{j}=x_{i}(1+\sum b_{\alpha\beta}x^{\alpha}y^{\beta})+h^{\prime}_{j}=gx_{i}+h^{\prime}_{j},

where
h^{\prime}_{j}:=\sum d_{\alpha\beta}x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}
, where all
x^{\alpha}
do not depend upon
x_{i}
, and

g:=1+\sum b_{\alpha\beta}x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}

is invariant invertible at
p
. Further shrinking
U
to
U_{g}
and introducing new invertible variable
y_{r+1}
on
G_{m}
we obtain the induced
T
-equivariant embedding

\phi:U_{g}\hookrightarrow W^{\prime}=W\times G_{m}=V\times{\mathbb{A}}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r+1}

such that
y_{r+1}\mapsto g
. This also introduces the additional invariant local parameter
v^{\prime\prime}_{j}:=y_{r+1}-g
, such that

•

v_{1},\ldots,v_{j-1},v^{\prime\prime}_{j},v^{\prime}_{j},v_{j+1},\ldots,v_{r}
is a system of local parameters describing
\phi(U)
on
W^{\prime}
.

•

v_{1},\ldots,v_{j-1},v^{\prime\prime}_{j}
are invariant
with their images in
m_{p}/(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})+m_{p}^{2}
are linearly independent.

Then
v_{j}^{\prime}
can be written modulo
v_{j}^{\prime\prime}
as

v_{j}^{\prime}=y_{r+1}x_{i}+h_{i}\sim x_{i}+h^{\prime}_{j}\cdot y_{r+1}^{-1}

Consider a
T^{l}
-equivariant
automorphism of
W^{\prime}
taking
x_{i}+h^{\prime}_{j}\cdot y_{r+1}^{-1}\mapsto x_{i}
, and
x_{j}\mapsto x_{j}
. Thus, after the coordinate change one can assume that the parameter
v^{\prime}_{j}=y_{r+1}x_{j}
. This induces the embedding: into

\phi^{\prime}:U_{g}\hookrightarrow W^{\prime\prime}:=V(x_{i})=U_{0}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{s-1}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r+1},

and proves Lemma
5.2.8
.
∎

Let
\phi:U{\hookrightarrow}W_{U}:=(V\times{\mathbf{A}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{d})
be the closed
T
-equivariant embedding as in Lemma
5.2.8
,
whose image is defined by the invariant parameters
v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}
such that their restrictions to closed subscheme
V\times\{0\}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{d}
also define the parameters.

Consider the splitting
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{d}={\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}
, where
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}=({\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{d})^{T}
is the maximal subtorus, such that
T
acts trivially on
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}
, and transitively on
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}
.
Denote by
y_{1},\ldots,y_{a}
the coordinates in
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}
, and by
z_{1},\ldots,z_{b}
the coordinates in
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}
.

Let

\pi:W:=V\times{\mathbb{A}}^{s}_{k}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{d}=V\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}\times({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})\to{\mathbb{A}}^{s}_{k}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}.

be the natural projection.
Denote by
q\in{\mathbb{A}}^{s}_{k}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}
the image
q=\pi(p)
, and by
p_{0}\in W/T
the image of
p
under the quotient
W\to W/T
.

Any invariant parameter
v_{j}
can be written as

v_{j}=\sum c^{j}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}z^{\gamma},

where
c^{j}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\in{\mathcal{O}}(V)
. Then for any
\gamma\neq 0
the action of
T
on
z^{\gamma}
is not trivial. Hence
\alpha\neq 0
as the action on
x^{\alpha}z^{\gamma}
is trivial .

This shows that

v_{j}=\sum c^{j}_{\beta}y^{\beta}+\sum_{\alpha\neq 0}c^{j}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}z^{\gamma}

Thus the restriction of
v_{j}
to the smooth fiber

F_{q}=\pi^{-1}(q)\simeq V\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}\times\operatorname{Spec}(k(q))\subset V\times\{0\}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{d}

determines the parameters on
F_{q}
defined by
\sum c^{j}_{\beta}y^{\beta}
.

Let
q_{0}\in{\mathbb{A}}^{s}_{k}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}/T
be the image of
q
.
The geometric quotient of
W
by
T
can be written as

W/T=U_{0}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}\times({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})/T=U_{0}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}\times X_{\sigma},

where

X_{\sigma}:=({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})/T

is a toric variety with the abelian quotient singularities.

The composition of morphisms

\psi:U_{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\hookrightarrow}}W=U_{0}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}\times({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}

induces

\psi/T:U_{Z}/T\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi/T}}{{\hookrightarrow}}W/T=U_{0}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a}\times X_{\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi/T}}{{\to}}X_{\sigma}=({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})/T,

for which the restrictions of
v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{Z}/T)={\mathcal{O}}(U_{Z})^{T}
at
p_{0}
to the fiber

(\pi/T)^{-1}(q_{0})=(V\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{a})\times\operatorname{Spec}(k(q_{0}))

also define regular parameters.

∎

Corollary 5.2.9
.

Let
X_{0}
be a smooth variety over a field
k
. Let
X
be obtained by a sequence of
m
cobordant blow-ups at smooth centers from
Y
, equivariant for the induced torus actions. Let
Z
be a regular
T
-stable closed subscheme of
X
, where
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}=\operatorname{Spec}(k[t,t^{-1}])
, and
T={\mathbb{G}}^{m}
.
Then the geometric quotient
Z/T
exists.
Moreover, locally there exists a smooth morphism

U_{Z}/T\to X_{\sigma}:=({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})/T,

where
U_{Z}\subset Z
is an open affine
T
-stable and
X_{\sigma}
is a toric variety over
k
with abelian quotient singularities.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.2.6
, and Proposition
5.2.7
, for any point
p_{0}\in Z/T
there is
a smooth morphism

{\pi/T}:W/T=(V\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})\times X_{\sigma}\to X_{\sigma},

and the closed immersion
U_{Z}/T\hookrightarrow W/T
, with the image defined by the functions
v_{1},\ldots,v_{r}\in{\mathcal{O}}(W)^{T}={\mathcal{O}}(W/T)
, and its restriction to the smooth fiber

({\pi/T})^{-1}(q_{0})\simeq U_{0}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b}\times\operatorname{Spec}(k(q_{0}))

determines a partial system of local parameters.
Thus, by
[
Sta
, Lemma 10.99.3.]
the morphism

\psi/T:U_{Z}/T\to X_{\sigma}:=({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{s}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{b})/T

is flat at
p_{0}
, and, by Lemma
[
Sta
, Lemma 29.34.14(2)]
, it is smooth at
p_{0}
.

∎

Remark 5.2.10
.

The above result is not valid in the mixed characteristic. The morphism
\pi/T
, which is a projection along regular scheme is not flat, in general, and one needs to work with the presentation from Proposition
5.2.7
directly:

U/T\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi/T}}{{\hookrightarrow}}V\times X_{\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi/T}}{{\to}}X_{\sigma},

where
V
is a regular scheme,
{\phi/T}
is a closed embedding, and
\pi/T
is the projection. Here the image
{\phi/T}(U/T)
is defined by local parameters at
p_{0}\in{\phi/T}(U/T)
whose restriction to the regular fiber
F_{q_{0}}=({\pi/T})^{-1}(q_{0})
, where
q_{0}=({\pi/T})(p_{0})
, are also parameters.

5.3.
Cobordant blow-ups in the logarithmic category

Let
X
be a logarithmically regular scheme, with Zariski logarithmic structure (or a strict toroidal variety) and assume that
{\mathcal{J}}
has a more general form

{\mathcal{J}}=(u_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,u_{k}^{{1/w_{k}}},m_{1}^{1/w_{k+1}},\ldots,m_{r}^{1/w_{k+r}}),

where
u_{i}
are free parameters and
m_{i}
are monomials for the logarithmic structure on
X
then the full cobordant blow-up
B\to X
at
{\mathcal{J}}

can be described as

B=\operatorname{Spec}({{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}[t^{-1},t^{w_{1}}{x_{1}},\ldots,t^{w_{k}}{x_{k}},t^{w_{k+1}}{m_{1}},\ldots,t^{w_{k+r}}{m_{r}}])^{int},

which is again a logarithmically regular scheme,(respectively strict toroidal variety). By functoriality, the construction extends to any logarithmically regular schemes (or toroidal varieties).

Initially, in the papers
[
ATW17
]
,
[
ATW20
]
, we considered the stack-theoretic blow-ups of the centers of the form
{\mathcal{J}}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k},m_{1}^{1/w_{k+1}},\ldots,m_{r}^{1/w_{k+r}})
, in the context of Kummer étale topology on the logarithmic stacks. Then in
[
ATW19
]
, we developed the formalism of the stack-theoretic blow-ups of the weighted centers of the form
(u_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,u_{k}^{{1/w_{k}}})
.

Soon after Quek in
[
Que20
, Section 3.2]
studied the stack-theoretic blow-ups of the more general form

{\mathcal{J}}=(u_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,u_{k}^{{1/w_{k}}},m_{1}^{1/w_{k+1}},\ldots,m_{r}^{1/w_{k+r}})=(u_{1}^{1/w_{1}},\ldots,u_{k}^{{1/w_{k}}},Q^{1/w})

in the context of logarithmic stacks and Kummer étale topology. Here
Q
is generated by
m_{i}^{w/w_{i}}
for the corresponding
w
.
